Interconnection Customer’s most recent Interconnection Study report and 
the Large Load Network Upgrade costs assigned to the Interconnection 
Request identified in that report have increased by more than twenty-five 
percent (25%) compared to costs identified in Large Load Interconnection 
Customer’s preceding Interconnection Study report; or (3) Large Load 
Interconnection Customer withdraws after receiving an Interconnection 
Study report issued after completion of the initial Large Load 
Interconnection Study and the Large Load Network Upgrade costs 
assigned to the Interconnection Request identified in such Interconnection
--- PAGE 109 ---
Southern California Edison Company 
FERC Electric Tariff, Third Revised Volume No. 6 
 
 
Study report have increased by more than one hundred percent (100%) 
compared to costs identified in the initial Large Load Interconnection 
Study report. 
 
3.10.1 Calculation of the Withdrawal Penalty 
If the Large Load Interconnection Customer withdraws its 
Interconnection Request or is deemed withdrawn prior to the 
commencement of the Large Load Interconnection Study, Large 
Load Interconnection Customer will not be subject to a Withdrawal 
Penalty.  If Large Load Interconnection Customer withdraws, is 
deemed withdrawn, or otherwise does not achieve its Service 
Commencement Date at any point after the commencement of the 
Large Load Interconnection Study, that Large Load Interconnection 
Customer’s Withdrawal Penalty will be the greater of: (1) the Large 
Load Interconnection Customer’s study deposit required under LLIP 
Section 4.2.1; or (2) as follows in (a)–(d): 
 
(a) If Large Load Interconnection Customer withdraws or is 
deemed withdrawn during the Large Load Interconnection Study 
or after receipt of a Large Load Interconnection Study report, 
Large Load Interconnection Customer will be charged two (2) 
times the actual cost of the Large Load Interconnection Study 
performed for the Large Load Interconnection Customer. 
 
(b) If Large Load Interconnection Customer withdraws or is 
deemed withdrawn after receipt of the draft TSRA but before 
Large Load Interconnection Customer has executed a TSRA or 
has requested that its TSRA be filed unexecuted, Large Load 
Interconnection Customer will be charged ten percent (10%) its 
estimated Large Load Network Upgrade costs as reflected in 
the most recently completed Interconnection Study.   
 
(c) If Large Load Interconnection Customer has executed a 
TSRA or has requested that its TSRA be filed unexecuted and 
subsequently withdraws its Interconnection Request, the Large 
Load Interconnection Customer’s Withdrawal Penalty will 
calculated as described in the TSRA.   
 
The Commercial Readiness Deposit shall be retained as financial 
security for the Withdrawal Penalty (if applicable) and other than as 
described in the TSRA, any security not applied to the Withdrawal 
Penalty will be released to the Interconnection Customer following 
withdrawal or the Service Commencement Date. 
 
3.11 Standards for Co-located Inverter Based Generating Facilities
--- PAGE 110 ---
Southern California Edison Company 
FERC Electric Tariff, Third Revised Volume No. 6 
 
 
 
Inverters used for the production, and/or later injection from storage, of 
electricity shall meet the inverter certification standards of UL-1741 and 
UL-1741 Supplement B. 
 
Section 4.  Large Load Study Process 
 
4.1 
Timing For Submitting Interconnection Requests 
A Large Load Interconnection Customer may submit an Interconnection 
Request for processing under the LLIP at any time during the year. The 
Participating TO, in coordination with the ISO, as applicable, will study the 
Interconnection Request.   
 
4.2 Processing of Interconnection Request 
 
4.2.1 
Initiating an Interconnection Request.  To initiate an 
Interconnection Request under the LLIP, Large Load 
Interconnection Customer must submit all of the following: (i) an 
Interconnection Study Deposit of $250,000; (ii) a completed 
Interconnection Request in the form of Attachment 1 to the LLIP, 
including preferred Point of Interconnection and voltage level, and 
all other technical data; (iii) a Commercial Readiness Deposit equal 
to two times the study deposit described in this Section 4.2.1 in the 
form of an irrevocable letter of credit, cash, a surety bond, or other 
form of security that is reasonably acceptable to Participating TO, in 
accordance with LLIP Section 4.7.1. This Commercial Readiness 
Deposit is refunded to Large Load Interconnection Customer 
according to LLIP Section 3.10.1; however, the Participating TO 
shall be entitled to hold the Commercial Readiness Deposit until the 
Large Load Interconnection Customer’s Generating Facility 
achieves its Service Commencement Date; and (iv) demonstration 
of Site Control.   
 
4.2.1.1 Use of Interconnection Study Deposit.  The 
Interconnection Study Deposit shall be applied to pay for 
prudent costs incurred by the Participating TO, the ISO, or 
third parties at the direction of the Participating TO or ISO, 
as applicable, to perform and administer the Interconnection 
Studies.   
 
In the event a Large Load Interconnection Customer 
withdraws its Interconnection Request prior to the 
commencement of the Large Load Interconnection Study, 
Large Load Interconnection Customer must pay Participating 
TO the actual costs of processing its Interconnection 
Request. In the event a Large Load Interconnection 
Customer withdraws after commencement of the Large Load
--- PAGE 111 ---
Southern California Edison Company 
FERC Electric Tariff, Third Revised Volume No. 6 
 
 
Interconnection Study, Participating TO shall charge and 
Large Load Interconnection Customer shall pay the actual 
costs of the Interconnection Studies.  
 
Any difference between the study deposit and the actual cost 
of the Interconnection Studies shall be paid by or refunded to 
the Large Load Interconnection Customer. Any invoices for 
Interconnection Studies shall include a detailed and itemized 
accounting of the cost of each Interconnection Study. Large 
Load Interconnection Customers shall pay any such 
undisputed costs within thirty (30) Calendar Days of receipt 
of an invoice. If Large Load Interconnection Customer fails to 
pay such undisputed costs within the time allotted, its 
Interconnection Request shall be deemed withdrawn and will 
be subject to Withdrawal Penalties pursuant to LLIP Section 
3.10.1. 
 
The Participating TO will reimburse the ISO or third parties, 
as applicable, for all work performed on behalf of the 
withdrawn Interconnection Request at the Participating TO’s 
direction. The Large Load Interconnection Customer must 
pay all monies due before it is allowed to obtain any 
Interconnection Study data or results. 
 
4.3 
Validation of Interconnection Request 
 
4.3.1 Acknowledgment of Interconnection Request.  Participating TO 
shall notify the Large Load Interconnection Customer within ten 
(10) Business Days of receipt of the Interconnection Request, 
which notice shall state whether the Interconnection Request is 
deemed valid.   
 
4.3.2 Deficiencies in Interconnection Request.  An Interconnection 
Request will not be considered to be a valid request until all items 
in LLIP Section 4.2.1 have been received by Participating TO and 
deemed valid by the Participating TO.  If an Interconnection 
Request fails to meet the requirements set forth in LLIP Section 
4.2.1, Participating TO shall include in its notification to the Large 
Load Interconnection Customer under LLIP Section 4.3.1 the 
reasons for such failure and that the Interconnection Request does 
not constitute a valid request.  Large Load Interconnection 
Customer shall provide Participating TO the additional requested 
information needed to constitute a valid request.  Whenever the 
additional requested information is provided by the Large Load 
Interconnection Customer, the Participating TO shall notify the 
Large Load Interconnection Customer within five (5) Business Days
--- PAGE 112 ---
Southern California Edison Company 
FERC Electric Tariff, Third Revised Volume No. 6 
 
 
of receipt of the additional requested information whether the 
Interconnection Request is valid.  If the Interconnection Request 
continues to fail to meet the requirements set forth in LLIP Section 
4.2.1, the Participating TO shall include in its notification to the 
Large Load Interconnection Customer the reasons for such failure.  
If an Interconnection Request has not been deemed valid, the 
Large Load Interconnection Customer must submit all information 
necessary to meet the requirements of LLIP Section 4.2.1 no later 
than twenty (20) Business Days after the date the original 
Interconnection Request was submitted, or ten (10) Business Days 
after the Participating TO first provided notice that the 
Interconnection Request was not valid, whichever is later.  
Interconnection Requests that have not met the requirements of 
LLIP Section 4.2.1 within twenty (20) Business Days after the date 
the original Interconnection Request was submitted or ten (10) 
Business Days after the Participating TO first provided notice that 
the Interconnection Request was not valid, whichever is later, will 
be deemed invalid and will not be included in the LLIP.  
 
Interconnection Requests deemed invalid under this LLIP Section 
4.3.2 are not subject to LLIP Section 3.9.  Large Load 
Interconnection Customers with invalid Interconnection Requests 
under this LLIP Section 4.3.2 may seek relief under LLIP Section 
7.2 by notifying the Participating TO within two (2) Business Days 
of the notice of invalidity. 
 
4.4      Potential Deferral of the Interconnection Studies 
           The Participating TO may defer commencement of the Large Load 
Interconnection Study when it determines, using Good Utility Practice and 
reasonable engineering judgment, that the Interconnection Request 
cannot be studied meaningfully until other pending study results for 
higher-queued projects are available. 
 
         Within ten (10) Business Days after deeming an Interconnection Request 
valid under Section 4.3.1, the Participating TO will notify the Large Load 
Interconnection Customer in writing if it is deferring the start of the 
Interconnection Studies. The notice will include: (i) information regarding 
the pending studies; and (ii) the estimated start date of the Large Load 
Interconnection Study.  During the period of deferral, the Participating TO 
will provide a monthly status update to the Interconnection Customer. 
 
4.5 
Scoping Meeting 
Within fifteen (15) Business Days after the Participating TO notifies the 
Large Load Interconnection Customer that the Interconnection Request 
has been deemed valid as set forth in LLIP Section 4.3.2, the Participating
--- PAGE 113 ---
Southern California Edison Company 
FERC Electric Tariff, Third Revised Volume No. 6 
 
 
TO shall establish a date agreeable to the Large Load Interconnection 
Customer, and the ISO, if applicable, for the Scoping Meeting.   
 
The purpose of the Scoping Meeting shall be to discuss reasonable 
Service Commencement Dates and alternative interconnection options, to 
exchange information including any transmission data that would 
reasonably be expected to impact such interconnection options, to 
analyze such information and to determine the potential feasible Points of 
Interconnection and eliminate alternatives given resources and available 
information. The Scoping Meeting shall also include discussion of a 
deferral of the Interconnection Studies in accordance with LLIP Section 
4.4.     
 
The Participating TO will bring to the meeting, as reasonably necessary to 
accomplish its purpose, such already available technical data, including, 
but not limited to, (i) general facility loadings, (ii) general instability issues, 
(iii) general short circuit issues, (iv) general voltage issues, and (v) general 
reliability issues.    
 
The Large Load Interconnection Customer will bring to the Scoping 
Meeting, in addition to the technical data in Appendix A to LLIP 
Attachment 1, any system studies previously performed.  The Participating 
TO, the ISO, if applicable, and the Large Load Interconnection Customer 
will also bring to the meeting personnel and other resources as may be 
reasonably required to accomplish the purpose of the meeting in the time 
allocated for the meeting.  On the basis of the meeting, the Large Load 
Interconnection Customer shall designate its Point of Interconnection.  
The duration of the meeting shall be sufficient to accomplish its purpose. 
 
Within five (5) Business Days after the Scoping Meeting, the Participating 
TO  shall provide the Large Load Interconnection Customer with a Large 
Load Study Process Agreement in the form set forth in LLIP Attachment 2, 
which shall contain an outline of the scope of the Large Load 
Interconnection Study, contain a non-binding good faith estimate of the 
cost to perform such study, and shall specify that the Large Load 
Interconnection Customer is responsible for the actual cost of the 
Interconnection Studies, including reasonable administrative costs.  The 
Large Load Interconnection Customer shall execute and deliver to the 
Participating TO the Large Load Study Process Agreement no later than 
thirty (30) Calendar Days after the Scoping Meeting, or the 
Interconnection Request shall be deemed withdrawn. 
 
4.6 
Interconnection Studies 
The Interconnection Studies shall consist of a Large Load Interconnection 
Study and any additional studies, including but not limited to those
--- PAGE 114 ---
Southern California Edison Company 
FERC Electric Tariff, Third Revised Volume No. 6 
 
 
identified in LLIP Section 4.6.3 required for interconnection of the Large 
Load, and co-located generation not exporting to the ISO Controlled Grid, 
to the Participating TO’s Transmission System.  The analysis of impacts 
on, and upgrades required to, the Participating TO’s Transmission System 
will be performed by the Participating TO and will be subject to the review 
and concurrence of the ISO in accordance with Section 25.7 of the ISO 
Tariff. The Interconnection Studies will identify Interconnection Facilities, 
Distribution Upgrades and required Large Load Network Upgrades 
necessary to mitigate thermal overloads and voltage violations, and 
address short circuit, stability, and reliability issues associated with the 
requested Transmission Interconnection Service. When a Large Load 
Owner is the Large Load Interconnection Customer, the Interconnection 
Studies will be limited to the identification of Large Load Network 
Upgrades necessary to mitigate thermal overloads and voltage violations, 
and address short circuit, stability, and reliability issues associated with 
the requested Transmission Interconnection Service.  
 
All cost estimates for Participating TO’s Interconnection Facilities, 
Distribution Upgrades, and Large Load Network Upgrades, as applicable, 
contained in the Interconnection Studies will be set forth in the 
Interconnection Study report in present dollar costs as well as time-
adjusted dollar costs, adjusted to the estimated year of construction of the 
components being constructed.   
 
The Participating TO will prepare an Interconnection Study report to 
document the results of the Interconnection Studies.   
 
4.6.1 Large Load Interconnection Study.  
 
 
4.6.1.1  Scope of the Large Load Interconnection Study.  The 
Large Load Interconnection Study will consist of a short 
circuit analysis, a power flow analysis, an electromagnetic 
transient (“EMT”) screening, a non-binding good faith 
estimate of the scope and cost of the Participating TO’s 
Interconnection Facilities, Distribution Upgrades and Large 
Load Network Upgrades, and any other studies that are 
deemed necessary. The Large Load Interconnection Study 
will also identify the need for any additional studies (e.g., 
stability analysis, EMT study, etc.) to be performed as part of 
the scope of the TSRA pursuant to LLIP Section 4.6.3.  
When there is co-located generation not exporting to the ISO 
Controlled Grid and not subject to electrical short circuit duty 
dependence, the Large Load Interconnection Study will
--- PAGE 115 ---
Southern California Edison Company 
FERC Electric Tariff, Third Revised Volume No. 6 
 
 
identify the impacts associated with the Large Load and any 
incremental impacts of the co-located generation.       
The Large Load Interconnection Study will consider the use 
of alternative transmission technologies including dynamic 
line ratings, advanced power flow control devices, advanced 
conductors, and transmission switching, when evaluating 
transmission solutions to accommodate new Large Loads 
and their associated transmission system impacts.  
The Large Load Interconnection Study shall state the 
assumptions upon which it is based, state the results of the 
analyses, and provide the requirement or potential 
impediments to providing the requested Transmission 
Interconnection Service, including an estimate of the cost 
and length of time that would be necessary to correct any 
problems identified in those analyses and implement the 
Interconnection.   
 
The Large Load Interconnection Study shall provide a list of 
Participating TO’s Interconnection Facilities, Distribution 
Upgrades, and Large Load Network Upgrades, as 
applicable, that are required as a result of the 
Interconnection Request. 
 
4.6.1.2 Timing of the Large Load Interconnection Study 
Results.  The Participating TO shall use Reasonable Efforts 
to complete the Large Load Interconnection Study within 
ninety (90) Calendar Days after the execution of a Large 
Load Study Process Agreement.  The Participating TO will 
issue a Large Load Interconnection Study report to the Large 
Load Interconnection Customer. The Participating TO will 
share study results with the ISO for review and concurrence.   
 
At any time the Participating TO determines that it will not 
meet the required time frame for completing the Large Load 
Interconnection Study, Participating TO shall notify the Large 
Load Interconnection Customer as to the schedule status of 
the Large Load Interconnection Study and provide an 
estimated completion date with an explanation of the 
reasons why additional time is required. 
 
Upon request, the Participating TO shall provide the Large 
Load Interconnection Customer all supporting 
documentation, workpapers and relevant pre-Interconnection
--- PAGE 116 ---
Southern California Edison Company 
FERC Electric Tariff, Third Revised Volume No. 6 
 
 
Request and post-Interconnection Request power flow and 
short circuit databases for the Large Load Interconnection 
Study, subject to confidentiality arrangements consistent 
with LLIP Section 7.1. 
 
Should the Large Load Interconnection Customer provide 
written comments on the Large Load Interconnection Study 
report at least five (5) Business Days before the Report 
Meeting conducted to discuss the report, the Participating 
TO will address the written comments in the Large Load 
Interconnection Study Report Meeting.  Should the Large 
Load Interconnection Customer provide comments at any 
later time (up to the time of the Report Meeting), then such 
comments shall be considered informal inquiries to which the 
Participating TO will provide informal, informational 
responses at the Report Meeting, to the extent possible.  
 
The Large Load Interconnection Customer may submit, in 
writing, additional comments on the final Large Load 
Interconnection Study report up to (3) Business Days 
following the Report Meeting.  Based on any discussion at 
the Report Meeting and any comments received from the 
Large Load Interconnection Customer or the ISO, the 
Participating TO will determine whether it is necessary to 
follow the Large Load Interconnection Study report with a 
revised study report.  The Participating TO will issue any 
such revised report to the Large Load Interconnection 
Customer no later than fifteen (15) Business Days following 
the Report Meeting. If the ISO has not completed its review 
of the Large Load Interconnection Study report, the 
Participating TO shall delay its determination of whether a 
revised study report is required until that review is complete. 
 
4.6.1.3 Large Load Interconnection Study Report Meeting.  A 
Report Meeting shall be held among the Participating TO, 
the ISO, if applicable, and the Large Load Interconnection 
Customer to discuss the results of the Large Load 
Interconnection Study, including assigned cost responsibility.  
A Report Meeting will be held within ten (10) Business Days 
of the date the Large Load Interconnection Study report is 
provided to the Large Load Interconnection Customer.  
 
4.6.1.4 Increase in the Commercial Readiness Deposit.  The 
Large Load Interconnection Customer shall submit an 
additional deposit that brings the total Commercial 
Readiness Deposit to twenty percent (20%) of Large Load
--- PAGE 117 ---
Southern California Edison Company 
FERC Electric Tariff, Third Revised Volume No. 6 
 
 
Interconnection Customer’s Large Load Network Upgrade 
cost assignment identified in the Large Load Interconnection 
Study, within thirty (30) Calendar Days after being provided 
with the final Large Load Interconnection Study report, or its 
Interconnection Request shall be deemed withdrawn.   
 
 
Should the Large Load Interconnection Study report be 
revised as the result of ISO review, in accordance with LLIP 
Section 4.6.1.2, the increase in the Commercial Readiness 
Deposit shall be adjusted to reflect twenty percent (20%) of 
the assigned Large Load Network Upgrades in the revised 
report within thirty (30) Calendar Days of issuance of the 
final report. 
   
4.6.1.5 Restudy. If restudy of the Large Load Interconnection 
Study is required due to a higher queued project dropping 
out of the queue, or a modification of a higher queued 
project subject to Section 4.6.1.6, Participating TO shall 
notify Large Load Interconnection Customer in writing. Such 
restudy shall take no longer than forty-five (45) Calendar 
Days from the date of notice. Any cost of restudy shall be 
borne by the Large Load Interconnection Customer. 
 
4.6.1.6 Modifications.  At any time during the course of the 
Interconnection Studies, the Large Load Interconnection 
Customer, the Participating TO, or the ISO, as applicable, 
may identify changes to the planned interconnection that 
may improve the costs and benefits (including reliability) of 
the interconnection, and the ability of the proposed change 
to accommodate the Interconnection Request.  To the extent 
the identified changes are acceptable to the Participating 
TO, the ISO, as applicable, and Large Load Interconnection 
Customer, such acceptance not to be unreasonably 
withheld, Participating TO shall modify the configuration in 
accordance with such changes without altering the 
Interconnection Request’s eligibility for participating in 
Interconnection Studies.          
 
4.6.1.6.1 For any modifications other than those agreed 
upon under LLIP Section 4.6.1.6, the Interconnection 
Customer must first request that Participating TO 
evaluate whether such modification is a Material 
Modification.  In response to Interconnection 
Customer's request, Participating TO, in coordination 
with the ISO, if applicable, shall evaluate the 
proposed modifications prior to making them and
--- PAGE 118 ---
Southern California Edison Company 
FERC Electric Tariff, Third Revised Volume No. 6 
 
 
inform Interconnection Customer in writing of whether 
the modifications would constitute a Material 
Modification.  The Participating TO may, at its option, 
engage the services of the ISO to assist in the 
assessment of the modification.  Any change to the 
Point of Interconnection shall constitute a Material 
Modification.   
 
 
 
The Large Load Interconnection Customer shall 
provide the Participating TO a $30,000 deposit for the 
modification assessment at the time the request is 
submitted.  Except as provided below, any 
modification assessment will be concluded, and a 
response provided to the Large Load Interconnection 
Customer in writing, within forty-five (45) Calendar 
Days from the date the Participating TO receives all of 
the following: 1) the Large Load Interconnection 
Customer’s written notice to modify the project; 2) a 
narrative description of the requested modification; 3) 
technical data required to assess the request, 
including an updated Appendix A to the 
Interconnection Request Form (LLIP Attachment 1), if 
requested by the Participating TO; 4) any additional 
information requested by Participating TO; and 5) 
payment of the $30,000 deposit.  If applicable, the 
Participating TO shall coordinate the modification 
request with the ISO.  If the modification assessment 
cannot be completed within that time period, the 
Participating TO shall notify the Large Load 
Interconnection Customer and provide an estimated 
completion date with an explanation of the reasons 
why additional time is required.  The Large Load 
Interconnection Customer will be responsible for the 
actual costs incurred by the Participating TO and, if 
applicable, the ISO in conducting the modification 
assessment.  If the actual costs of the modification 
assessment are less than the deposit provided by the 
Large Load Interconnection Customer, the Large 
Load Interconnection Customer will be refunded the 
balance within thirty (30) Calendar Days of being 
invoiced.  If the actual costs of the modification 
assessment are greater than the deposit provided by 
the Large Load Interconnection Customer, the Large 
Load Interconnection Customer shall pay the balance 
within thirty (30) Calendar Days of being invoiced.
--- PAGE 119 ---
Southern California Edison Company 
FERC Electric Tariff, Third Revised Volume No. 6 
 
 
If the proposed modification is determined to be a 
Material Modification by the Participating TO pursuant 
to the modification assessment described above, the 
Large Load Interconnection Customer shall then 
either:  
 
(i) 
withdraw the proposed modification, or  
 
(ii) 
withdraw the proposed modification and submit a 
new Interconnection Request reflecting such 
modification. 
 
4.6.2    Optional Flexible Interim Load Interconnection (FILI) Study 
and Agreement. Based on the results of the Large Load 
Interconnection Study, the Large Load Interconnection Customer 
may elect to have a FILI Study performed when the estimated 
completion date of the PTO’s Interconnection Facilities, 
Distribution Upgrades, or Large Load Network Upgrades identified 
in the Large Load Interconnection Study is beyond the Large Load 
Interconnection Customer’s requested Service Commencement 
Date.  
 
 
The Large Load Interconnection Customer’s election to proceed 
with the FILI Study must be made by written notification to the 
Participating TO within fifteen (15) Business Days of the Large 
Load Interconnection Study Report Meeting.  
 
The Participating TO will perform the FILI Study within sixty (60) 
Calendar Days of receipt of the Large Load Interconnection 
Customer’s written notification. The Large Load Interconnection 
Customer will be responsible for the actual costs of the Study. At 
any time Participating TO determines that it will not meet the 
required time frame for completing the FILI Study, Participating TO 
shall notify the Large Load Interconnection Customer as to the 
schedule status of the FILI Study and provide an estimated 
completion date with an explanation of the reasons why additional 
time is required. 
 
The FILI study will determine: (i) whether a Large Load 
Interconnection Customer is eligible to receive non-firm limited 
load capacity; (ii) the conditions and limitations of such service; (iii) 
the upgrades and the associated cost, and (iv) the estimated 
duration required to provide the limited service. The Participating 
TO will share study results with the ISO for review and 
concurrence.  If requested by the Large Load Interconnection 
Customer within ten (10) Business Days of the date of the
--- PAGE 120 ---
Southern California Edison Company 
FERC Electric Tariff, Third Revised Volume No. 6 
 
 
issuance of the FILI Study report, a Report Meeting shall be 
scheduled among the Participating TO, the ISO, and the Large 
Load Interconnection Customer. 
 
 
 
The Large Load Interconnection Customer should note the 
following considerations: 
a) 
Temporary limits may be adjusted if unforeseen conditions 
arise. 
b) 
Advance notice of non-firm load curtailment may not be 
possible. 
c) 
Non-firm load may be curtailed during planned or repair 
outages, which may last longer than 6 months. 
d) 
ISO may have additional operational requirements. 
e) 
Participating TO is not obligated to provide flexibility beyond 
what it can, in its sole judgment, manage operationally. 
f) 
Interconnection Facilities costs for interim interconnection 
service will be directly assigned to the Large Load 
Interconnection Customer. 
g) 
Large Load Interconnection Customers not adhering to the 
contractual agreement and conditions may be curtailed 
indefinitely.  
 
4.6.2.1 FILI Agreement with Participating TO and ISO. If the 
Large Load Interconnection Customer has elected Flexible 
Interim Load Interconnection (FILI), as reflected in the 
TSRA, the Large Load Interconnection Customer will be 
required to execute an agreement with the Participating TO 
and ISO, in the form of agreement included in Attachment 4 
to LLIP, prior to the Service Commencement Date, in 
accordance with ISO Tariff Section 25.7.2. The agreement 
shall describe the circumstances in which the ISO or 
Participating TO may temporarily curtail the Large Load 
receiving FILI, and the mechanism the ISO or Participation 
TO will use to perform the curtailment. The ISO will post 
such FILI agreements on the ISO Website.  
 
4.6.3   Additional Studies. The Participating TO may identify the need for 
additional studies to comply with Applicable Reliability Standards. 
These additional studies may include, but not limited to, the 
following: stability analysis, variable load impact analysis, and EMT 
analysis.  The associated new scope will be identified in the Large 
Load Interconnection Study pursuant to LLIP Section 4.6.1.1. The 
additional studies will be performed by the Participating TO as part 
of the scope of work set forth in the Transmission Security and 
Reliability Agreement (“TSRA”). The additional studies may result in
--- PAGE 121 ---
Southern California Edison Company 
FERC Electric Tariff, Third Revised Volume No. 6 
 
 
the identification of additional facilities scope and cost, and may 
require a revision to the schedule set forth in the TSRA. The Large 
Load Interconnection Customer will be responsible for providing the 
necessary technical information and/or models requested by the 
Participating TO to conduct the additional studies. The Large Load 
Interconnection Customer will be responsible for the costs of such 
studies.            
 
If an additional study results in the need to revise the scope of work 
of the Large Load Network Upgrades identified in the Large Load 
Interconnection Study and the updated assigned costs have 
increased by more than twenty-five percent (25%) compared to 
costs identified in the Large Load Interconnection Study Report, the 
Large Load Interconnection Customer shall not be subject to a 
Withdrawal Penalty under LLIP Section 3.10.1 if the Interconnection 
Request is withdrawn within thirty (30) Calendar Days of receipt of 
the additional study report.  If the Large Load Interconnection 
Customer elects to continue with its Interconnection Request, the 
TSRA will be amended to reflect the results of the additional study. 
 
4.6.4   Financing of Participating TO’s Interconnection Facilities, 
Distribution Upgrades and Large Load Network Upgrades.  The 
responsibility to finance Participating TO’s Interconnection Facilities 
and Distribution Upgrades shall be assigned solely to the Large 
Load Interconnection Customer, except where the Large Load will 
be a retail transmission customer of Participating TO, in which case 
the local regulatory authority will determine any financing 
responsibilities for Distribution Upgrades. The Participating TO will 
be responsible for financing Large Load Network Upgrades in 
accordance with the TSRA.   
 
4.7 
Interconnection Financial Security   
 
4.7.1 Types of Interconnection Financial Security.  The 
Interconnection Financial Security posted by a Large Load 
Interconnection Customer may be any combination of the following 
types of Interconnection Financial Security provided in favor of the 
Participating TO: 
 
(a) 
an irrevocable and unconditional letter of credit issued by a 
bank or financial institution that has a credit rating of A or 
better by Standard and Poor’s or A2 or better by Moody’s;  
 
(b) 
an irrevocable and unconditional surety bond issued by an 
insurance company that has a credit rating of A or better by 
Standard and Poor’s or A2 or better by Moody’s;
--- PAGE 122 ---
Southern California Edison Company 
FERC Electric Tariff, Third Revised Volume No. 6 
 
 
(c) 
an unconditional and irrevocable guaranty issued by a 
company has a credit rating of A or better by Standard and 
Poor’s or A2 or better by Moody’s; 
 
(d) 
a cash deposit standing to the credit of the Participating TO 
and in an interest-bearing escrow account maintained at a 
bank or financial institution that is reasonably acceptable to 
the Participating TO; 
 
(e) 
a certificate of deposit in the name of the Participating TO 
issued by a bank or financial institution that has a credit 
rating of A or better by Standard and Poor’s or A2 or better 
by Moody’s; or  
 
(f) 
a payment bond certificate in the name of the Participating 
TO issued by a bank or financial institution that has a credit 
rating of A or better by Standard and Poor’s or A2 or better 
by Moody’s.  
 
Interconnection Financial Security instruments as listed above shall 
be in such form as the Participating TO may reasonably require 
from time to time by notice to Large Load Interconnection 
Customers, or in such other form as has been evaluated and 
approved as reasonably acceptable by the Participating TO.     
 
The Participating TO shall require the use of standardized forms of 
Interconnection Financial Security to the greatest extent possible.  
If at any time the guarantor of the Interconnection Financial 
Security fails to maintain the credit rating required by this LLIP 
Section 4.7.1, the Large Load Interconnection Customer shall 
provide to the Participating TO replacement Interconnection 
Financial Security meeting the requirements of this LLIP Section 
4.7.1 within five (5) Business Days of the change in credit rating.  
 
Interest on a cash deposit standing to the credit of the Participating 
TO in an interest-bearing escrow account under subpart (d) of this 
LLIP Section 4.7.1 will accrue to the Large Load Interconnection 
Customer’s benefit.  
 
Execution and filing of the TSRA at FERC are addressed in LLIP 
Section 5. 
 
Section 5. 
Transmission Security and Reliability Agreement  
 
5.1 
Tender.  Within thirty (30) Calendar Days after the Report Meeting for the final 
Large Load Interconnection Study report, or the final FILI Study report, if
--- PAGE 123 ---
Southern California Edison Company 
FERC Electric Tariff, Third Revised Volume No. 6 
 
 
applicable, the Participating TO shall tender a draft TSRA, together with 
draft appendices.  The draft TSRA shall be in the form of Participating 
TO's FERC-approved form of TSRA, which is in Attachment 3 to the LLIP.  
The Large Load Interconnection Customer shall provide written 
comments, or notification of no comments, to the draft appendices within 
thirty (30) Calendar Days. 
 
5.2 
Negotiation.  Notwithstanding LLIP Section 5.1, at the request of Large Load 
Interconnection Customer Participating TO shall begin negotiations with 
Large Load Interconnection Customer concerning the appendices to the 
TSRA at any time after the Participating TO provides the Large Load 
Interconnection Customer with the final Large Load Interconnection Study 
report, or the final FILI Study report, if applicable.  Participating TO and 
Large Load Interconnection Customer shall negotiate concerning any 
disputed provisions of the appendices to the draft TSRA for not more than 
sixty (60) Calendar Days after the Participating TO provides the Large 
Load Interconnection Customer with the final Large Load Interconnection 
Study report or the final FILI Study report if applicable.  If Large Load 
Interconnection Customer determines that negotiations are at an impasse, 
it may request termination of the negotiations at any time after tender of 
the draft TSRA pursuant to LLIP Section 5.1 and request submission of 
the unexecuted TSRA with FERC or initiate Dispute Resolution 
procedures pursuant to LLIP Section 7.2.  If Large Load Interconnection 
Customer requests termination of the negotiations, but within sixty (60) 
Calendar Days after issuance of the final Large Load Interconnection 
Study report, or the final FILI Study report if applicable, fails to request 
either the filing of the unexecuted TSRA or initiate Dispute Resolution, it 
shall be deemed to have withdrawn its Interconnection Request.  Unless 
otherwise agreed by the Parties, if Large Load Interconnection Customer 
has not executed the TSRA, requested filing of an unexecuted TSRA, or 
initiated Dispute Resolution procedures pursuant to LLIP Section 7.2 
within sixty (60) Calendar Days after issuance of the final Large Load 
Interconnection Study report, or the final FILI Study report if applicable, it 
shall be deemed to have withdrawn its Interconnection Request.  
Participating TO shall provide to Large Load Interconnection Customer a 
final TSRA within fifteen (15) Business Days after the completion of the 
negotiation process. 
 
 
The Participating TO may declare an impasse upon sixty (60) Calendar 
Days after issuance of the final Large Load Interconnection Study report, 
or the final FILI Study report if applicable, or at anytime following sixty (60) 
Calendar Days after issuance of the final Large Load Interconnection 
Study report, or the final FILI Study report if applicable, if the Parties have 
agreed to extend negotiation of the TSRA.  If the Participating TO declares 
an impasse, the Participating TO will file the TSRA unexecuted with FERC 
within twenty one (21) Calendar Days.
--- PAGE 124 ---
Southern California Edison Company 
FERC Electric Tariff, Third Revised Volume No. 6 
 
 
 
5.3 
Execution and Filing 
Large Load Interconnection Customer shall either: (i) execute two originals 
of the tendered TSRA and return them to Participating TO; or (ii) request 
in writing that Participating TO file with FERC a TSRA in unexecuted form.  
As soon as practicable, but not later than ten (10) Business Days after 
receiving either the two executed originals of the tendered TSRA (if either 
does not conform with a FERC-approved standard form of TSRA) or the 
request to file an unexecuted TSRA, Participating TO shall file the TSRA 
with FERC, together with its explanation of any matters as to which Large 
Load Interconnection Customer and Participating TO disagree and 
support for the costs that Participating TO proposes to charge to Large 
Load Interconnection Customer under the TSRA.  An unexecuted TSRA 
should contain terms and conditions deemed appropriate by Participating 
TO for the Interconnection Request.  If the Parties agree to proceed with 
design, procurement, and construction of facilities and upgrades under the 
agreed-upon terms of the unexecuted TSRA, they may proceed pending 
FERC action. 
 
 
5.4 
Commencement of Interconnection Activities 
If Large Load Interconnection Customer executes the final TSRA, 
Participating TO and Large Load Interconnection Customer shall perform 
their respective obligations in accordance with the terms of the TSRA, 
subject to modification by FERC.  Upon submission of an unexecuted 
TSRA, Large Load Interconnection Customer and Participating TO shall 
promptly comply with the unexecuted TSRA, if agreed to by the Parties 
pursuant to LLIP Section 5.2, subject to modification by FERC. 
 
5.5 
Large Load Interconnection Customer To Meet Requirements of the 
Participating TO’s Interconnection Handbook   
The Large Load Interconnection Customer’s Interconnection Facilities 
shall be designed, constructed, operated and maintained in accordance 
with the Participating TO’s Interconnection Handbook.  In the event of a 
conflict between the terms of the LLIP and the terms of the Participating 
TO’s Interconnection Handbook, the terms in the LLIP shall govern.  
 
Section 6. 
Construction of Participating TO's Interconnection Facilities, 
Distribution Upgrades, and Large Load Network Upgrades  
 
 
6.1 
Schedule 
Participating TO and Large Load Interconnection Customer shall negotiate 
in good faith concerning a schedule for the Participating TO’s construction 
of Participating TO's Interconnection Facilities, Distribution Upgrades, and 
the Large Load Network Upgrades.
--- PAGE 125 ---
Southern California Edison Company 
FERC Electric Tariff, Third Revised Volume No. 6 
 
 
 
6.2 
Construction Sequencing 
 
6.2.1 General.  In general, the Service Commencement Date will 
determine the sequence of construction of Distribution Upgrades 
and Large Load Network Upgrades. 
 
6.2.2 Advancing Construction of Distribution Upgrades and Large 
Load Network Upgrades that are Part of an Expansion Plan of 
the Participating TO.  A Large Load Interconnection Customer 
with a TSRA, in order to maintain its Service Commencement Date, 
may request that Participating TO advance to the extent necessary 
the completion of Distribution Upgrades and Large Load Network 
Upgrades that:  (i) are necessary to support such Service 
Commencement Date and (ii) would otherwise not be completed, 
pursuant to an expansion plan of Participating TO or approved ISO 
Transmission Plan covering the Participating TO’s service territory, 
in time to support such Service Commencement Date.  Upon such 
request, Participating TO will use Reasonable Efforts to advance 
the construction of such Distribution Upgrades and Large Load 
Network Upgrades to accommodate such request; provided that 
Large Load Interconnection Customer commits to pay Participating 
TO any associated expediting costs.   
 
6.3 
Special Provisions for Affected Systems   
The Large Load Interconnection Customer shall enter into an agreement 
with the Affected System Operator, as applicable.  The agreement shall 
specify the terms governing payments to be made by the Large Load 
Interconnection Customer to the Affected System Operator.   
 
Section 7. 
Miscellaneous 
 
7.1 
Confidentiality 
 
For the purposes of this LLIP Section 7.1, “Party” or “Parties” shall mean 
the Participating TO, Large Load Interconnection Customer, ISO, or any 
combination of the Participating TO, Large Load Interconnection 
Customer, or ISO.  
 
Confidential Information shall include, without limitation, all information 
relating to a Party's technology, research and development, business 
affairs, and pricing. 
 
Information is Confidential Information only if it is clearly designated or 
marked in writing as confidential on the face of the document, or, if the 
information is conveyed orally or by inspection, if the Party providing the
--- PAGE 126 ---
Southern California Edison Company 
FERC Electric Tariff, Third Revised Volume No. 6 
 
 
information orally informs the Party receiving the information that the 
information is confidential. 
 
If requested by either Party, the other Party shall provide in writing, the 
basis for asserting that the information referred to in this Article warrants 
confidential treatment, and the requesting Party may disclose such writing 
to the appropriate Governmental Authority.  Each Party shall be 
responsible for the costs associated with affording confidential treatment 
to its information. 
 
 
 
7.1.1 Scope.  Confidential Information shall not include information that 
the receiving Party can demonstrate: (1) is generally available to 
the public other than as a result of a disclosure by the receiving 
Party; (2) was in the lawful possession of the receiving Party on a 
non-confidential basis before receiving it from the disclosing Party; 
(3) was supplied to the receiving Party without restriction by a third 
party, who, to the knowledge of the receiving Party after due 
inquiry, was under no obligation to the disclosing Party to keep 
such information confidential; (4) was independently developed by 
the receiving Party without reference to Confidential Information of 
the disclosing Party; (5) is, or becomes, publicly known, through no 
wrongful act or omission of the receiving Party or Breach of the 
TSRA; or (6) is required, in accordance with LLIP Section 7.1.6, 
Order of Disclosure, to be disclosed by any Governmental Authority 
or is otherwise required to be disclosed by law or subpoena, or is 
necessary in any legal proceeding establishing rights and 
obligations under the TSRA. Information designated as Confidential 
Information will no longer be deemed confidential if the Party that 
designated the information as confidential notifies the other Party 
that it no longer is confidential. 
 
7.1.2  Release of Confidential Information.  Neither Party shall release 
or disclose Confidential Information to any other person, except to 
its Affiliates (limited by the Standards of Conduct requirements), 
employees, consultants, Affected System Operators, or to parties 
who may be or considering providing financing to or equity 
participation with Large Load Interconnection Customer, or to 
potential purchasers or assignees of Large Load Interconnection 
Customer, on a need-to-know basis in connection with these 
procedures, unless such person has first been advised of the 
confidentiality provisions of this LLIP Section 7.1 and has agreed to 
comply with such provisions.  Notwithstanding the foregoing, a 
Party providing Confidential Information to any person shall remain 
primarily responsible for any release of Confidential Information in 
contravention of this LLIP Section 7.1.
--- PAGE 127 ---
Southern California Edison Company 
FERC Electric Tariff, Third Revised Volume No. 6 
 
 
7.1.3 Rights.  Each Party retains all rights, title, and interest in the 
Confidential Information that each Party discloses to the other 
Party.  The disclosure by each Party to the other Party of 
Confidential Information shall not be deemed a waiver by either 
Party or any other person or entity of the right to protect the 
Confidential Information from public disclosure. 
 
7.1.4 No Warranties.  By providing Confidential Information, neither 
Party makes any warranties or representations as to its accuracy or 
completeness.  In addition, by supplying Confidential Information, 
neither Party obligates itself to provide any particular information or 
Confidential Information to the other Party nor to enter into any 
further agreements or proceed with any other relationship or joint 
venture. 
 
7.1.5 Standard of Care.  Each Party shall use at least the same 
standard of care to protect Confidential Information it receives as it 
uses to protect its own Confidential Information from unauthorized 
disclosure, publication or dissemination.  Each Party may use 
Confidential Information solely to fulfill its obligations to the other 
Party under these procedures or its regulatory requirements. 
 
7.1.6 Order of Disclosure.  If a court or a Government Authority or entity 
with the right, power, and apparent authority to do so requests or 
requires either Party, by subpoena, oral deposition, interrogatories, 
requests for production of documents, administrative order, or 
otherwise, to disclose Confidential Information, that Party shall 
provide the other Party with prompt notice of such request(s) or 
requirement(s) so that the other Party may seek an appropriate 
protective order or waive compliance with the terms of these 
confidentiality provisions. Notwithstanding the absence of a 
protective order or waiver, the Party may disclose such Confidential 
Information which, in the opinion of its counsel, the Party is legally 
compelled to disclose.  Each Party will use Reasonable Efforts to 
obtain reliable assurance that confidential treatment will be 
accorded any Confidential Information so furnished. 
 
7.1.7 Remedies.  The Parties agree that monetary damages would be 
inadequate to compensate a Party for the other Party's Breach of 
its obligations under this LLIP Section 7.1.  Each Party accordingly 
agrees that the other Party shall be entitled to equitable relief, by 
way of injunction or otherwise, if the first Party Breaches or 
threatens to Breach its obligations under this LLIP Section 7.1, 
which equitable relief shall be granted without bond or proof of 
damages, and the receiving Party shall not plead in defense that 
there would be an adequate remedy at law.  Such remedy shall not
--- PAGE 128 ---
Southern California Edison Company 
FERC Electric Tariff, Third Revised Volume No. 6 
 
 
be deemed an exclusive remedy for the Breach of this LLIP Section 
7.1 but shall be in addition to all other remedies available at law or 
in equity.  The Parties further acknowledge and agree that the 
covenants contained herein are necessary for the protection of 
legitimate business interests and are reasonable in scope.  No 
Party, however, shall be liable for indirect, incidental, or 
consequential or punitive damages of any nature or kind resulting 
from or arising in connection with this LLIP Section 7.1. 
 
7.1.8 Disclosure to FERC, its Staff, or a State.  Notwithstanding 
anything in this LLIP Section 7.1 to the contrary, and pursuant to 18 
CFR section 1b.20, if FERC or its staff, during the course of an 
investigation or otherwise, requests information from one of the 
Parties that is otherwise required to be maintained in confidence 
pursuant to the LLIP, the Party shall provide the requested 
information to FERC or its staff, within the time provided for in the 
request for information.  In providing the information to FERC or its 
staff, the Party must, consistent with 18 CFR section 388.112, 
request that the information be treated as confidential and non-
public by FERC and its staff and that the information be withheld 
from public disclosure.  Parties are prohibited from notifying the 
other Party prior to the release of the Confidential Information to 
FERC or its staff.  The Party shall notify the other Party to the 
TSRA when it is notified by FERC or its staff that a request to 
release Confidential Information has been received by FERC, at 
which time either of the Parties may respond before such 
information would be made public, pursuant to 18 CFR section 
388.112.  Requests from a state regulatory body conducting a 
confidential investigation shall be treated in a similar manner, 
consistent with applicable state rules and regulations. 
 
7.1.9 Subject to the exception in LLIP Section 7.1.8, any information that 
a Party claims is competitively sensitive, commercial or financial 
information ("Confidential Information") shall not be disclosed by 
the other Party to any person not employed or retained by the other 
Party, except to the extent disclosure is (i) required by law; (ii) 
reasonably deemed by the disclosing Party to be required to be 
disclosed in connection with a dispute between or among the 
Parties, or the defense of litigation or dispute; (iii) otherwise 
permitted by consent of the other Party, such consent not to be 
unreasonably withheld;  or (iv) necessary to fulfill its obligations 
under this LLIP or as a transmission service provider or a Balancing 
Authority Area operator including disclosing the Confidential 
Information to an RTO or ISO or to a subregional, regional or 
national reliability organization or planning group.  The Party 
asserting confidentiality shall notify the other Party in writing of the
--- PAGE 129 ---
Southern California Edison Company 
FERC Electric Tariff, Third Revised Volume No. 6 
 
 
information it claims is confidential.  Prior to any disclosures of the 
other Party's Confidential Information under this subparagraph, or if 
any third party or Governmental Authority makes any request or 
demand for any of the information described in this subparagraph, 
the disclosing Party agrees to promptly notify the other Party in 
writing and agrees to assert confidentiality and cooperate with the 
other Party in seeking to protect the Confidential Information from 
public disclosure by confidentiality agreement, protective order or 
other reasonable measures. 
 
7.1.10 This provision shall not apply to any information that was or is 
hereafter in the public domain (except as a result of a Breach of this 
provision). 
 
7.1.11 Participating TO shall, at Large Load Interconnection Customer's 
election, destroy, in a confidential manner, or return the 
Confidential Information provided at the time of Confidential 
Information is no longer needed. 
 
 
7.2 
Disputes  
 
 
 
7.2.1  Submission.  In the event either Party has a dispute, or asserts a 
claim, that arises out of or in connection with the TSRA, the LLIP, 
or their performance, such Party (the "disputing Party") shall 
provide the other Party with written notice of the dispute or claim 
("Notice of Dispute").  Such dispute or claim shall be resolved in 
accordance with the Dispute Resolution Procedures set forth in 
Section 14 of the TO Tariff.

Southern California Edison Company 
FERC Electric Tariff, Third Revised Volume No. 6

14. 
Load Flow and Dynamic Models: 
 
The WECC Data Preparation Manual for Power Flow Base Cases and Dynamic Stability 
Data has established power flow and dynamic modeling requirements for generation 
projects in WECC base cases.  In general, if the aggregate sum of generation on a bus 
exceeds 10 MVA, it should not be netted.  Furthermore, the total netted generation in an 
area should not exceed five percent of the area’s total generation.  Based on current 
WECC modeling requirements, the following information will be required for all 
generation projects whose net capacity is greater than 10 MVA.  The following 
information may also be required for generation projects less than 10 MVA on a case-
by-case basis, based on the amount of generation in the area of the requested Point of 
Interconnection.  Each Large Load shall provide the load flow and dynamic model data 
specified in Item C of this section, in addition to the information in Items A and B for any 
co-located generation. 
 
A.   
Provide load flow model for the generating plant and its interconnection 
facilities in GE PSLF *.epc format, including new buses, generators, 
transformers, interconnection facilities. An equivalent model is required for 
the plant with generation collector systems.  This data should reflect the 
technical data provided in this Attachment A. 
 
B. 
For each generator, governor, exciter, power system stabilizer, WTG, or 
inverter based generator, select the appropriate dynamic models from the 
General Electric PSLF Program Manual and provide the required input 
data. Include any user written *.p EPCL files to simulate inverter based 
plants’ dynamic responses (typically needed for inverter based PV/wind 
plants).  Provide a completed *.dyd file that contains the information 
specified in this section.   
 
C.   For Large Loads, the Large Load Interconnection Customer shall provide a 
validated user-defined root mean squared (RMS) positive sequence 
dynamics model and an appropriately parameterized generic library RMS 
positive sequence dynamics model, including model block diagram of the 
inverter control and plant control systems, as defined by a model 
approved by the Western Electricity Coordinating Council, that
--- PAGE 150 ---
Southern California Edison Company 
FERC Electric Tariff, Third Revised Volume No. 6  
 
corresponds to Large Load Interconnection Customer’s Generating 
Facility. Refer to the PSLF User Manual and available NERC 
documentation on the use and parameterization of model parameters. 
Enter “N/A” for any field that is not applicable (e.g., distributed generation 
data). If multiple motors or Type 1 loads need to be represented, copy the 
relevant fields and identify the applicable parameters. 
 
The GE PSLF manual is available upon request from GE.  There are links within the GE 
PSLF User’s Manual to detailed descriptions of specific models, a definition of each 
parameter, a list of the output channels, explanatory notes, and a control system block 
diagram.  In addition, GE PSLF modeling information and various modeling guidelines 
documents have been prepared by the WECC Modeling and Validation Work Group.  
This information is available on the WECC website (www.wecc.org).  
 
If you require assistance in developing the models, we suggest you contact GE 
Vernova. Accurate models are important to obtain accurate study results. Costs 
associated with any changes in facility requirements that are due to differences between 
model data provided by the generation developer and the actual generator test data, 
may be the responsibility of the generation developer. 
 
15.       MODELS FOR NON-SYNCHRONOUS GENERATORS 
For a non-synchronous Generating Facility, Large Load Interconnection Customer shall 
provide (1) a validated user-defined root mean squared (RMS) positive sequence 
dynamics model; (2) an appropriately parameterized generic library RMS positive 
sequence dynamics model, including model block diagram of the inverter control and 
plant control systems, as defined by a model approved by the Western Electricity 
Coordinating Council, that corresponds to Large Load Interconnection Customer’s 
Generating Facility; and (3) if applicable, a validated electromagnetic transient model if 
Distribution Provider performs an electromagnetic transient study as part of the 
interconnection study process.  A user-defined model is a set of programming code 
created by equipment manufacturers or developers that captures the latest features of 
controllers that are mainly software based and represents the entities’ control strategies 
but does not necessarily correspond to any generic library model.  Large Load 
Interconnection Customer must also demonstrate that the model is validated by 
providing evidence that the equipment behavior is consistent with the model behavior 
(e.g., an attestation from Large Load Interconnection Customer that the model 
accurately represents the entire Generating Facility; attestations from each equipment 
manufacturer that the user defined model accurately represents the component of the 
Generating Facility; or test data).
--- PAGE 151 ---
Southern California Edison Company 
FERC Electric Tariff, Third Revised Volume No. 6

RECITALS 
 
 
WHEREAS, Large Load Interconnection Customer is proposing to develop a 
Large Load or  capacity addition to an existing Large Load consistent with the 
Interconnection Request submitted by Large Load Interconnection Customer dated 
____                    ; and 
 
 
WHEREAS, Large Load Interconnection Customer desires to interconnect the 
Large Load  with the Participating TO’s Transmission System pursuant to the LLIP; and 
 
WHEREAS, the Large Load Interconnection Customer has requested 
Participating TO to perform Interconnection Studies to assess the system impact of 
interconnecting the Large Load to the Participating TO’s Transmission System, and any 
Affected Systems and to specify and estimate the cost of the equipment, engineering, 
procurement and construction work needed on the Participating TO’s electric system to 
physically and electrically connect the Large Load to the Participating TO’s 
Transmission System in accordance with Good Utility Practice; 
 
 
NOW, THEREFORE, in consideration of and subject to the mutual covenants 
contained herein the Parties agree as follows: 
 
 
1.0 
When used in this Agreement, with initial capitalization, the terms 
specified shall have the meanings indicated in Participating TO’s FERC 
approved LLIP. 
 
 
2.0 
Large Load Interconnection Customer elects and Participating TO shall 
cause to be performed Interconnection Studies consistent with Section 4 
of the LLIP.
--- PAGE 152 ---
Southern California Edison Company 
FERC Electric Tariff, Third Revised Volume No. 6  
 
 
3.0 
The scope of the Interconnection Studies shall be subject to the 
assumptions set forth in Appendix A to this Agreement. 
 
 
4.0 
The Interconnection Studies will be based upon the technical information 
provided by Large Load Interconnection Customer in the Interconnection 
Request, as may be modified as the result of the Scoping Meeting, subject 
to any modifications in accordance with LLIP Section 4.6.1.6.  
Participating TO reserves the right to request additional technical 
information from Large Load Interconnection Customer as may reasonably 
become necessary consistent with Good Utility Practice during the course 
of the Interconnection Studies.  If Large Load Interconnection Customer 
modifies its designated Point of Interconnection, Interconnection Request, 
or the technical information provided therein is modified, the 
Interconnection Studies may be modified as specified in the LLIP. 
 
 
5.0 
The Interconnection Study report for each Interconnection Study shall 
provide the information specified in the LLIP. 
 
6.0 
Large Load Interconnection Customer shall increase the Commercial 
Readiness Deposit after issuance of the final Large Load Interconnection  
Study report in accordance with LLIP Section 4.6.1.4.  
 
 
7.0 
Upon completion of the Interconnection Studies, Participating TO shall 
charge and Large Load Interconnection Customer shall pay the actual 
costs of the Interconnection Studies pursuant to LLIP Section 3.2.3.3. 
 
8. 0     The Participating TO may provide copies of the Interconnection Studies 
results to the ISO, an Affected System Operator and the Western 
Electricity Coordinating Council.  Requests for review and input from any 
Affected System Operators or the Western Electricity Coordinating Council 
may arrive at any time prior to interconnection. 
 
9.0 
Substantial portions of technical data and assumptions used to perform 
the Interconnection Studies, such as system conditions, existing and 
planned generation, and unit modeling, may change after the Participating 
TO provides the Interconnection Studies results to the Large Load 
Interconnection Customer.  Interconnection Studies results will reflect 
available data at the time the Participating TO provides the 
Interconnection Study reports to the Large Load Interconnection 
Customer.  The Participating TO shall not be responsible for any 
additional costs for Participating TO’s  Interconnection Facilities, 
Distribution Upgrades and Large Load Network Upgrades, including, 
without limitation, costs of new or additional facilities, system upgrades, or 
schedule changes, that may be incurred by the Large Load 
Interconnection Customer as a result of changes in such data and 
assumptions.
--- PAGE 153 ---
Southern California Edison Company 
FERC Electric Tariff, Third Revised Volume No. 6  
 
 
10.0 The Participating TO shall maintain records and accounts of all costs 
incurred in performing the Interconnection Studies in sufficient detail to 
allow verification of all costs incurred, including associated overheads.  
The Large Load Interconnection Customer shall have the right, upon 
reasonable notice, within a reasonable time at the Participating TO’s 
offices and at its own expense, to audit the Participating TO’s records as 
necessary and as appropriate in order to verify costs incurred by the 
Participating TO.  Any audit requested by the Large Load Interconnection 
Customer shall be completed, and written notice of any audit dispute 
provided to the Participating TO, within one hundred eighty (180) Calendar 
Days following receipt by the Large Load Interconnection Customer of the 
Participating TO’s  notification of the final costs of the Interconnection 
Studies. 
 
11.0 In accordance with LLIP Section 3.11, the Large Load Interconnection 
Customer may withdraw its Interconnection Request at any time by written 
notice to the Participating TO.  Upon receipt of such notice, this 
Agreement shall terminate, subject to the requirements of LLIP Sections 
4.2.1.1 and 7.1. 
 
12.0 This Agreement shall become effective upon the date the fully executed 
Agreement is received by the Participating TO.  If the Participating TO 
does not receive the fully executed Agreement pursuant to LLIP Section 
4.4, then the Interconnection Request will be deemed withdrawn upon the 
Large Load Interconnection Customer’s receipt of written notice by the 
Distribution Provider pursuant to LLIP Section 3.11.

TRANSMISSION SECURITY AND RELIABILITY AGREEMENT 
 
 
 
THIS TRANSMISSION SECURITY AND RELIABILITY AGREEMENT (“TSRA” 
or “Agreement”) is made and entered into ______________, by and between 
_______________________, a ____________________________ organized and 
existing under the laws of the State/Commonwealth of ________________ (“Large 
Load Interconnection Customer” with a Large Load), and Southern California Edison 
Company, a corporation organized and existing under the laws of the State of California 
(“Participating TO”).  Large Load Interconnection Customer and Participating TO each 
may be referred to as a “Party” or collectively as the “Parties.” 
 
Recitals 
 
 
WHEREAS, Participating TO owns the Participating TO’s Transmission System; 
and  
 
 
WHEREAS, Large Load Interconnection Customer and Participating TO have 
agreed to enter into this Agreement for the purpose of defining certain transmission 
security and reliability terms necessary to interconnect the Large Load with the 
Participating TO’s Transmission System; 
 
 
NOW, THEREFORE, in consideration of and subject to the mutual covenants 
contained herein, it is agreed: 
 
Article 1. 
Definitions 
 
Terms used in this TSRA with initial capitalization shall have the meanings set forth 
below.  The singular of any definition shall include the plural and the plural shall include 
the singular.  If a term with initial capitalization used herein is not defined, such term 
shall have the meanings ascribed to such term in the LLIP and Section 3 of the TO 
Tariff. 
 
 
 
Affiliate shall mean, with respect to a corporation, partnership or other entity, 
each such other corporation, partnership or other entity that directly or indirectly, 
through one or more intermediaries, controls, is controlled by, or is under common 
control with, such corporation, partnership or other entity. 
 
 
Ancillary Services shall mean those services that are necessary to support the 
transmission of capacity and energy from resources to loads while maintaining reliable 
operation of the Participating TO’s Transmission System and Distribution System in 
accordance with Good Utility Practice. 
 
 
Breach shall mean the failure of a Party to perform or observe any material term 
or condition of the TSRA.
--- PAGE 167 ---
Southern California Edison Company 
FERC Electric Tariff, Third Revised Volume No. 6 
 
 
Breaching Party shall mean a Party that is in Breach of the TSRA. 
 
 
Customer-Financed Monthly Rate for Non-ISO-Controlled Facilities shall 
have the meaning as defined in Appendix X of the Transmission Owner Tariff.  The 
currently effective Customer-Financed Monthly Rate for Non-ISO-Controlled Facilities is 
as provided in Appendix X of the Transmission Owner Tariff. 
 
 
Default shall mean the failure of a Breaching Party to cure its Breach in 
accordance with Article 17 of the TSRA. 
 
 
Distribution Upgrades Charge shall mean the monthly charge to the Large 
Load Interconnection Customer to recover the revenue requirements for the 
Participating TO’s Distribution Upgrades, calculated as the product of the Customer-
Financed Monthly Rate for Non-ISO-Controlled Facilities and the Distribution Upgrades 
Cost.  The Distribution Upgrades Charge is provided in Section 5 of Appendix A to the 
TSRA.  The Distribution Upgrades Charge is not applicable to Large Load 
Interconnection Customers who are retail transmission customers of Participating TO.  
 
 
Distribution Upgrades Completion Date shall mean the date upon which the 
construction of the Distribution Upgrades is complete and such facilities are successfully 
tested and ready for service. 
 
 
Distribution Upgrades Cost shall mean the Large Load Interconnection 
Customer’s allocated share of all costs, excluding One-Time Cost, determined by the 
Participating TO to be associated with the design, engineering, procurement, 
construction and installation of the Distribution Upgrades.  The Distribution Upgrades 
Cost is provided in Section 5 of Appendix A to the TSRA. 
 
Emergency Condition shall mean a condition or situation: (1) that in the 
judgment of the Party making the claim is imminently likely to endanger life or property; 
or (2) that, in the case of the ISO, is imminently likely (as determined in a non-
discriminatory manner) to cause a material adverse effect on the security of, or damage 
to, the ISO Controlled Grid or the electric systems of others to which the ISO Controlled 
Grid is directly connected; (3) that, in the case of the Participating TO, is imminently 
likely (as determined in a non-discriminatory manner) to cause a material adverse effect 
on the security of, or damage to, the Participating TO’s Transmission System, 
Participating TO’s Interconnection Facilities, Distribution System, or the electric systems 
of others to which the Participating TO’s electric system is directly connected; or (4) 
that, in the case of the Large Load Interconnection Customer, is imminently likely (as 
determined in a non-discriminatory manner) to cause a material adverse effect on the 
security of, or damage to, the Large Load or Large Load Interconnection Customer’s 
Interconnection Facilities.  System restoration shall be considered an Emergency 
Condition. 
 
 
Hazardous Substances shall mean any chemicals, materials or substances 
defined as or included in the definition of “hazardous substances,” “hazardous wastes,”

“hazardous materials,” “hazardous constituents,” “restricted hazardous materials,”

material or substance, exposure to which is prohibited, limited or regulated by any 
applicable Environmental Law. 
 
 
Interconnection Facilities Charge shall mean the monthly charge to the Large

IRS shall mean the Internal Revenue Service.

Metering Equipment shall mean all metering equipment installed or to be 
installed at the Large Load and co-located generation, if applicable, pursuant to the 
TSRA at the metering points, including but not limited to instrument transformers, MWh-
meters, data acquisition equipment, transducers, remote terminal unit, communications 
equipment, phone lines, and fiber optics. 
 
 
One-Time Cost shall mean all costs determined by the Participating TO to be 
associated with the installation of the Participating TO’s Interconnection Facilities and 
Distribution Upgrades which are not capitalized.  The One-Time Cost is provided in 
Appendix A to the TSRA. 
 
Party or Parties shall mean Participating TO, Large Load Interconnection 
Customer or any combination of the above. 
 
 
Remedial Action Scheme (RAS) shall mean a scheme designed to detect 
predetermined system conditions and automatically take corrective actions that may 
include, but are not limited to, adjusting or tripping load or generation. 
 
 
Service Commencement Date shall mean the date the Large Load is initially 
operated in parallel with the Participating TO’s Transmission System and transmission 
interconnection service is provided to the Large Load Interconnection Customer 
pursuant to the terms of an executed TSRA.
