Uncontrollable Force shall mean any act of God, labor disturbance, act of the 
public enemy, war, insurrection, riot, fire, storm, flood, earthquake, explosion, breakage 
or accident to machinery or equipment, any curtailment, order, regulation or restriction 
imposed by governmental, military or lawfully established civilian authorities, or any 
other cause beyond the reasonable control of the Participating TO or Large Load 
Interconnection Customer which could not be avoided through the exercise of Good 
Utility Practice.  An Uncontrollable Force event does not include acts of negligence or 
intentional wrongdoing by the Party claiming Uncontrollable Force. 
 
Article 2. 
Effective Date, Term, and Termination 
 
2.1 
Effective Date.  This TSRA shall become effective upon execution by the Parties 
subject to acceptance by FERC (if applicable), or if filed unexecuted, upon the 
date specified by FERC.  Participating TO shall promptly file this TSRA with 
FERC upon execution in accordance with Article 3.1, if required. 
 
2.2 
Term of Agreement.  Subject to the provisions of Article 2.3, this TSRA shall 
remain in effect for a period of fifteen (15) years from the Effective Date. 
 
2.3 
Termination Procedures. 
 
 
2.3.1 Written Notice.  This TSRA may be terminated by Large Load 
Interconnection Customer after giving Participating TO ninety (90) 
Calendar Days advance written notice, or by Participating TO notifying 
FERC after the Large Load permanently ceases operation. 
 
 
2.3.2 Default.  Either Party may terminate this TSRA in accordance with Article 
17. 
 
2.3.3 Notwithstanding Articles 2.3.1 and 2.3.2, no termination shall become 
effective until the Parties have complied with all Applicable Laws and 
Regulations applicable to such termination, including the filing with FERC 
of a notice of termination of this TSRA, which notice has been accepted 
for filing by FERC, and the Large Load Interconnection Customer has 
fulfilled its termination cost obligations under Article 2.4. 
  
2.4 
Termination Costs.  If a Party elects to terminate this Agreement pursuant to 
Article 2.3 above, each Party shall pay all costs incurred (including any cancellation 
costs relating to orders or contracts for Interconnection Facilities and equipment) or 
charges assessed by the other Party, as of the date of the other Party’s receipt of such 
notice of termination, that are the responsibility of the Terminating Party under this 
TSRA.  In the event of termination by a Party, the Parties shall use commercially 
Reasonable Efforts to mitigate the costs, damages and charges arising as a 
consequence of termination.  Upon termination of this TSRA, unless otherwise ordered 
or approved by FERC:
--- PAGE 170 ---
Southern California Edison Company 
FERC Electric Tariff, Third Revised Volume No. 6 
 
 
2.4.1 With respect to any portion of Participating TO’s Interconnection Facilities 
that have not yet been constructed or installed, Participating TO shall to 
the extent possible and with Large Load Interconnection Customer’s 
authorization cancel any pending orders of, or return, any materials or 
equipment for, or contracts for construction of, such facilities; provided that 
in the event Large Load Interconnection Customer elects not to authorize 
such cancellation, Large Load Interconnection Customer shall assume all 
payment obligations with respect to such materials, equipment, and 
contracts, and Participating TO shall deliver such material and equipment, 
and, if necessary, assign such contracts, to Large Load Interconnection 
Customer as soon as practicable, at Large Load Interconnection 
Customer’s expense.  To the extent that Large Load Interconnection 
Customer has already paid Participating TO for any or all such costs of 
materials or equipment not taken by Large Load Interconnection 
Customer, Participating TO shall promptly refund such amounts to Large 
Load Interconnection Customer, less any costs, including penalties 
incurred by Participating TO to cancel any pending orders of or return 
such materials, equipment, or contracts. 
 
If a Large Load Interconnection Customer terminates this TSRA, it shall be 
responsible for all costs incurred in association with that Large Load 
Interconnection Customer’s interconnection, including any cancellation 
costs relating to orders or contracts for Interconnection Facilities and 
equipment, and other expenses including any Distribution Upgrades and 
Large Load Network Upgrades for which Participating TO has incurred 
expenses and has not been reimbursed by Large Load Interconnection 
Customer. 
 
 
2.4.2 Participating TO may, at its option, retain any portion of such materials, 
equipment, or facilities that Large Load Interconnection Customer chooses 
not to accept delivery of, in which case Participating TO shall be 
responsible for all costs associated with procuring such materials, 
equipment, or facilities. 
 
 
2.4.3 With respect to any portion of the Interconnection Facilities, and any other 
facilities already installed or constructed pursuant to the terms of this 
TSRA, Large Load Interconnection Customer shall be responsible for all 
costs associated with the removal, relocation or other disposition or 
retirement of such materials, equipment, or facilities. 
 
 
2.4.4 Large Load Interconnection Customer will also be responsible for any 
Withdrawal Fee or Exit Fee under Appendix B to this TSRA. 
 
2.5 
Disconnection.  Upon termination of this TSRA, the Parties will take all 
appropriate steps to disconnect the Large Load from the Participating TO’s 
Transmission System.  All costs required to effectuate such disconnection shall
--- PAGE 171 ---
Southern California Edison Company 
FERC Electric Tariff, Third Revised Volume No. 6 
 
be borne by the terminating Party, unless such termination resulted from the non-
terminating Party’s Default of this TSRA or such non-terminating Party otherwise 
is responsible for these costs under this TSRA. 
 
2.6 
Survival.  This TSRA shall continue in effect after termination to the extent 
necessary to provide for final billings and payments and for costs incurred 
hereunder, including billings and payments pursuant to this TSRA; to permit the 
determination and enforcement of liability and indemnification obligations arising 
from acts or events that occurred while this TSRA was in effect; and to permit 
each Party to have access to the lands of the other Party pursuant to this TSRA 
or other applicable agreements, to disconnect, remove or salvage its own 
facilities and equipment. 
 
Article 3. 
Regulatory Filings 
 
3.1 
Filing.  Participating TO shall file this TSRA (and any amendment hereto) with 
FERC and any other appropriate Governmental Authority, if required.  Large 
Load Interconnection Customer may request that any information provided be 
subject to the confidentiality provisions of Article 22.  If Large Load 
Interconnection Customer has executed this TSRA, or any amendment thereto, 
Large Load Interconnection Customer shall reasonably cooperate with 
Participating TO with respect to such filing and to provide any information 
reasonably requested by Participating TO needed to comply with applicable 
regulatory requirements. 
 
Article 4. 
Scope of Service 
 
4.1 
Transmission Interconnection Service.  Transmission Interconnection Service 
allows Large Load Interconnection Customer to connect the Large Load to the 
Participating TO’s Transmission System and be eligible to receive power from 
the ISO Grid, using the capacity of the ISO Controlled Grid.  To the extent Large 
Load Interconnection Customer wants to receive Transmission Interconnection 
Service, Participating TO shall construct facilities identified in Appendices A and 
D that the Participating TO is responsible to construct. 
 
4.1.1 Transmission Service Implications. Transmission Interconnection 
Service does not necessarily provide Large Load Interconnection 
Customer with the capability to physically receive power from the ISO 
Controlled Grid without incurring congestion costs.  In the event of 
transmission constraints on the ISO Controlled Grid, the Large Load 
Interconnection Customer shall be subject to the applicable congestion 
management procedures in the ISO Tariff in the same manner as all other 
Large Loads.  The Large Load Interconnection Customer shall be solely 
responsible for completing all of the necessary arrangements required 
under the ISO Tariff to be eligible to schedule the power required for the
--- PAGE 172 ---
Southern California Edison Company 
FERC Electric Tariff, Third Revised Volume No. 6 
 
Large Load, except when the Large Load will be a retail transmission 
customer of the Participating TO. 
 
4.2 
Provision of Service.  Participating TO shall provide Transmission 
Interconnection Service for the Large Load at the Point of Interconnection. 
 
4.3 
Performance Standards.  Each Party shall perform all of its obligations under 
this TSRA in accordance with Applicable Laws and Regulations, Applicable 
Reliability Standards, and Good Utility Practice, and to the extent a Party is 
required or prevented or limited in taking any action by such regulations and 
standards, such Party shall not be deemed to be in Breach of this TSRA for its 
compliance therewith.  If such Party is a Participating TO, then that Party shall 
amend the TSRA and submit the amendment to FERC for approval. 
 
4.4 
No Transmission Service.  The execution of this TSRA does not entitle the 
Large Load Interconnection Customer to transmission service under the ISO 
Tariff and does not convey any right to the Large Load Interconnection Customer 
to receive electricity using the ISO Controlled Grid.   
 
Article 5. 
Interconnection Facilities Engineering, Procurement, and 
Construction 
 
5.1 
Participating TO shall design, procure, and construct Participating TO’s 
Interconnection Facilities, Distribution Upgrades, and Large Load Network 
Upgrades, using Reasonable Efforts to complete Participating TO’s 
Interconnection Facilities, Distribution Upgrades, and Large Load Network 
Upgrades by the dates set forth in Appendix C.  Participating TO shall not be 
required to undertake any action which is inconsistent with its standard safety 
practices, its material and equipment specifications, its design criteria and 
construction procedures, its labor agreements, and Applicable Laws and 
Regulations.  In the event Participating TO reasonably expects that it will not be 
able to complete Participating TO’s Interconnection Facilities, Distribution 
Upgrades, and Large Load Network Upgrades by the specified dates, 
Participating TO shall promptly provide written notice to Large Load 
Interconnection Customer and shall undertake Reasonable Efforts to meet the 
earliest dates thereafter.  
 
5.2 
Equipment Procurement.  The Participating TO shall commence design of 
Participating TO’s Interconnection Facilities, Distribution Upgrades, or Large 
Load Network Upgrades and procure necessary equipment as soon as 
practicable after all of the following conditions are satisfied, unless the Parties 
otherwise agree in writing: 
 
 
5.2.1 Participating TO has completed the Interconnection Studies pursuant to 
the LLIP, except for any additional studies performed pursuant to this 
TSRA and in accordance with LLIP Section 4.6.3;

Southern California Edison Company 
FERC Electric Tariff, Third Revised Volume No. 6 
 
 
 
5.2.2 Participating TO has received written authorization to proceed with design 
and procurement from Large Load Interconnection Customer by the date 
specified in Appendix C; and 
 
 
5.2.3 Large Load Interconnection Customer has provided security to 
Participating TO in accordance with Article 11.4 by the dates specified in 
Appendix C. 
 
5.3 
Construction Commencement.  Participating TO shall commence construction 
of Participating TO’s Interconnection Facilities, Distribution Upgrades, and Large 
Load Network Upgrades for which it is responsible as soon as practicable after 
the following additional conditions are satisfied: 
 
 
5.3.1 Approval of the appropriate Governmental Authority has been obtained for 
any facilities requiring regulatory approval;

TO’s Interconnection Facilities, Distribution Upgrades, and Large Load 
Network Upgrades; 
 
 
5.3.3 Participating TO has received written authorization to proceed with 
construction from Large Load Interconnection Customer by the date 
specified in Appendix C; and 
 
 
5.3.4 Large Load Interconnection Customer has provided security to 
Participating TO in accordance with Article 11.4 by the dates specified in 
Appendix C. 
 
5.4 
Work Progress.  The Parties will keep each other advised periodically as to the 
progress of their respective design, procurement and construction efforts.  Either 
Party may, at any time, request a progress report from the other Party.  If, at any 
time, Large Load Interconnection Customer determines that the completion of 
Participating TO’s Interconnection Facilities will not be required until after the 
specified Service Commencement Date, Large Load Interconnection Customer 
will provide written notice to Participating TO of such later date upon which the 
completion of Participating TO’s Interconnection Facilities will be required. 
 
5.5 
Information Exchange.  As soon as reasonably practicable after the Effective 
Date, the Parties shall exchange information regarding the design and 
compatibility of the Parties’ Interconnection Facilities and compatibility of the 
Interconnection Facilities with Participating TO’s Transmission System, and shall 
work diligently and in good faith to make any necessary design changes.

5.6 
Large Load Interconnection Customer’s Interconnection Facilities.  Large

Participating TO at least one hundred eighty (180) Calendar Days prior to

to ensure that the Large Load Interconnection Customer’s Interconnection

control, and safety requirements of Participating TO and comment on such 
specifications within thirty (30) Calendar Days of Large Load 
Interconnection Customer’s submission.  All specifications provided 
hereunder shall be deemed confidential. 
 
 
5.6.2 Participating TO’s Review.  Participating TO’s review of Large Load 
Interconnection Customer’s final specifications shall not be construed as 
confirming, endorsing, or providing a warranty as to the design, fitness, 
safety, durability or reliability of the Large Load, or the Large Load 
Interconnection Customer’s Interconnection Facilities.  Large Load 
Interconnection Customer shall make such changes to the Large Load 
Interconnection Customer’s Interconnection Facilities as may reasonably 
be required by Participating TO, in accordance with Good Utility Practice, 
to ensure that the Large Load Interconnection Customer’s Interconnection 
Facilities are compatible with the technical specifications, operational 
control, and safety requirements of Participating TO. 
 
 
5.6.3 Large Load Interconnection Customer’s Interconnection Facilities

Interconnection Facilities shall be designed and constructed in accordance 
with Good Utility Practice.  Within one hundred twenty (120) Calendar 
Days after the Service Commencement Date, unless the Parties agree on 
another mutually acceptable deadline, Large Load Interconnection 
Customer shall deliver to Participating TO “as-built” drawings, information 
and documents for the Large Load Interconnection Customer’s 
Interconnection Facilities and Large Load, such as: a one-line diagram, a 
site plan showing the Large Load, co-located generation, if applicable, and 
the Large Load Interconnection Customer’s Interconnection Facilities, plan 
and elevation drawings showing the layout of the Large Load 
Interconnection Customer’s Interconnection Facilities, a relay functional 
diagram, relaying AC and DC schematic wiring diagrams and relay
--- PAGE 175 ---
Southern California Edison Company 
FERC Electric Tariff, Third Revised Volume No. 6 
 
settings for all facilities associated with Large Load Interconnection 
Customer’s transformers, the facilities connecting the Large Load and co-
located generation, if applicable, to the  transformers and the Large Load 
Interconnection Customer’s Interconnection Facilities, and the 
impedances (determined by factory tests) for the associated transformers 
and the Large Load and co-located generation, if applicable.  The Large 
Load Interconnection Customer shall provide Participating TO

5.7 
Participating TO’s Interconnection Facilities Construction.  Participating 
TO’s Interconnection Facilities shall be designed and constructed in accordance 
with Good Utility Practice.   
 
5.8 
Access Rights.  Upon reasonable notice and supervision by a Party, and 
subject to any required or necessary regulatory approvals, a Party (“Granting 
Party”) shall furnish at no cost to the other Party (“Access Party”) any rights of 
use, licenses, rights of way and easements with respect to lands owned or 
controlled by the Granting Party, its agents (if allowed under the applicable 
agency agreement), or any Affiliate, that are necessary to enable the Access 
Party to obtain ingress and egress to construct, operate, maintain, repair, test (or 
witness testing), inspect, replace or remove facilities and equipment to: (i) 
interconnect the Large Load with the Participating TO’s Transmission System; (ii) 
operate and maintain the Large Load, the Interconnection Facilities and the 
Participating TO’s Transmission System; and (iii) disconnect or remove the 
Access Party’s facilities and equipment upon termination of this TSRA.  In 
exercising such licenses, rights of way and easements, the Access Party shall 
not unreasonably disrupt or interfere with normal operation of the Granting 
Party’s business and shall adhere to the safety rules and procedures established 
in advance, as may be changed from time to time, by the Granting Party and 
provided to the Access Party.   
 
5.9 
Permits.  Participating TO and Large Load Interconnection Customer shall 
cooperate with each other in good faith in obtaining all permits, licenses and 
authorizations that are necessary to accomplish the interconnection in 
compliance with Applicable Laws and Regulations.

5.10 Early Construction of Base Case Facilities.  The Large Load Interconnection

Large Load Network Upgrades required for Large Load Interconnection

which are included in the Base Case of the Large Load Interconnection Study for 
Large Load Interconnection Customer, and which also are required to be 
constructed for another project, but where such construction is not scheduled to

Load Network Upgrades to accommodate such request, provided that Large 
Load Interconnection Customer commits to pay Participating TO any associated 
expediting costs.   
 
5.11 Taxes. 
 
 
5.11.1 Large Load Interconnection Customer Payments Taxable.  The 
Parties acknowledge and agree that all payments or property transfers 
made by Large Load Interconnection Customer to Participating TO for the 
installation of Participating TO’s Interconnection Facilities, Distribution 
Upgrades, and, if applicable, Large Load Network Upgrades will be 
taxable to the Participating TO under the Internal Revenue Code and 
applicable state income tax laws. Participating TO will include in the 
amounts charged to Large Load Interconnection Customer a gross-up for 
the income tax cost consequences of such payments or property 
transfers, calculated in accordance with Article 5.11.2 (Tax Gross-Up 
Amount) and subject to Article 5.11.3 (Private Letter Ruling or Change or 
Clarification of Law) and Article 5.11.4 (Refund).   
 
 
5.11.2 Tax Gross-Up Amount.  Participating TO will include in the amounts it 
charges Large Load Interconnection Customer a gross-up for the income 
tax cost consequences of the payments and property the former receives 
from the latter. Large Load Interconnection Customer’s liability for the 
income tax cost consequences of such payments or property transfers 
under this Article 5.11 shall be calculated on a fully grossed-up basis.  
Except as may otherwise be agreed to by the parties, this means that 
Large Load Interconnection Customer will pay Participating TO, in addition 
to the amount paid for the Interconnection Facilities, Distribution 
Upgrades, and Large Load Network Upgrades, an amount equal to (1) the 
current taxes imposed on Participating TO (“Current Taxes”) on the 
excess of (a) the gross income realized by Participating TO as a result of 
payments or property transfers made by Large Load Interconnection 
Customer to Participating TO under this TSRA (without regard to any 
payments under this Article 5.11) (the “Gross Income Amount”) over (b) 
the present value of future tax deductions for depreciation that will be
--- PAGE 177 ---
Southern California Edison Company 
FERC Electric Tariff, Third Revised Volume No. 6 
 
available as a result of such payments or property transfers (the “Present 
Value Depreciation Amount”), plus (2) an additional amount sufficient to 
permit Participating TO to receive and retain, after the payment of all 
Current Taxes, an amount equal to the net amount described in clause 
(1). 
 
For this purpose, (i) Current Taxes shall be computed based on 
Participating TO’s composite federal and state tax rates at the time the 
payments or property transfers are received and Participating TO will be 
treated as being subject to tax at the highest marginal rates in effect at 
that time (the “Current Tax Rate”), and (ii) the Present Value Depreciation 
Amount shall be computed by discounting Participating TO’s anticipated 
tax depreciation deductions as a result of such payments or property 
transfers by Participating TO’s current weighted average cost of capital.  
Thus, the formula for calculating Large Load Interconnection Customer’s 
liability to Participating TO pursuant to this Article 5.11.2 can be expressed 
as follows: (Current Tax Rate x (Gross Income Amount – Present Value of 
Tax Depreciation))/(1-Current Tax Rate).  Large Load Interconnection 
Customer’s estimated tax liability in the event taxes are imposed shall be 
stated in Appendix A. 
 
 
5.11.3 Private Letter Ruling or Change or Clarification of Law.  At Large Load 
Interconnection Customer’s request and expense, Participating TO shall 
file with the IRS a request for a private letter ruling as to whether any 
property transferred or sums paid, or to be paid, by Large Load 
Interconnection Customer to Participating TO under this TSRA are subject 
to federal income taxation.  Large Load Interconnection Customer will 
prepare the initial draft of the request for a private letter ruling, and will 
certify under penalties of perjury that all facts represented in such request 
are true and accurate to the best of Large Load Interconnection 
Customer’s knowledge.  Participating TO and Large Load Interconnection 
Customer shall cooperate in good faith with respect to the submission of 
such request. 
 
Participating TO shall keep Large Load Interconnection Customer fully 
informed of the status of such request for a private letter ruling and shall 
execute either a privacy act waiver or a limited power of attorney, in a form 
acceptable to the IRS, that authorizes Large Load Interconnection 
Customer to participate in all discussions with the IRS regarding such 
request for a private letter ruling.  Participating TO shall allow Large Load 
Interconnection Customer to attend all meetings with IRS officials about 
the request and shall permit Large Load Interconnection Customer to 
prepare the initial drafts of any follow-up letters in connection with the 
request.
--- PAGE 178 ---
Southern California Edison Company 
FERC Electric Tariff, Third Revised Volume No. 6 
 
 
 
5.11.4 Refund.  In the event that (a) a private letter ruling is issued to 
Participating TO which holds that any amount paid or the value of any 
property transferred by Large Load Interconnection Customer to 
Participating TO under the terms of this TSRA is not subject to federal 
income taxation, (b) any legislative change or administrative 
announcement, notice, ruling or other determination makes it reasonably 
clear to Participating TO in good faith that any amount paid or the value of 
any property transferred by Large Load Interconnection Customer to 
Participating TO under the terms of this TSRA is not taxable to 
Participating TO, (c) any abatement, appeal, protest, or other contest 
results in a determination that any payments or transfers made by Large 
Load Interconnection Customer to Participating TO are not subject to 
federal income tax, or (d) if Participating TO receives a refund from any 
taxing authority for any overpayment of tax attributable to any payment or 
property transfer made by Large Load Interconnection Customer to 
Participating TO pursuant to this TSRA, Participating TO shall promptly 
refund to Large Load Interconnection Customer the following: 
 
(i) any payment made by Large Load Interconnection Customer 
under this Article 5.11 for taxes that is attributable to the amount 
determined to be non-taxable, together with interest thereon, 
 
(ii) interest on any amounts paid by Large Load Interconnection 
Customer to Participating TO for such taxes which Participating TO 
did not submit to the taxing authority, calculated in accordance with 
the methodology set forth in FERC’s regulations at 18 CFR 
§35.19a(a)(2)(iii) from the date payment was made by Large Load 
Interconnection Customer to the date Participating TO refunds such 
payment to Large Load Interconnection Customer, and 
 
(iii) with respect to any such taxes paid by Participating TO, any 
refund or credit Participating TO receives or to which it may be 
entitled from any Governmental Authority, interest (or that portion 
thereof attributable to the payment described in clause (i), above) 
owed to Participating TO for such overpayment of taxes (including 
any reduction in interest otherwise payable by Participating TO to 
any Governmental Authority resulting from an offset or credit); 
provided, however, that Participating TO will remit such amount 
promptly to Large Load Interconnection Customer only after and to 
the extent that Participating TO has received a tax refund, credit or 
offset from any Governmental Authority for any applicable 
overpayment of income tax related to Participating TO’s 
Interconnection Facilities.
--- PAGE 179 ---
Southern California Edison Company 
FERC Electric Tariff, Third Revised Volume No. 6 
 
The intent of this provision is to leave the Parties, to the extent practicable, 
in the event that no taxes are due with respect to any payment for 
Interconnection Facilities, Distribution Upgrades, and Large Load Network 
Upgrades hereunder, in the same position they would have been in had 
no such tax payments been made. 
 
 
5.11.5 Taxes Other Than Income Taxes.  Upon the timely request by Large 
Load Interconnection Customer, and at Large Load Interconnection 
Customer’s sole expense, Participating TO may appeal, protest, seek 
abatement of, or otherwise contest any tax (other than federal or state 
income tax) asserted or assessed against Participating TO for which 
Large Load Interconnection Customer may be required to reimburse 
Participating TO under the terms of this TSRA.  Large Load 
Interconnection Customer shall pay to Participating TO on a periodic 
basis, as invoiced by Participating TO, Participating TO’s documented 
reasonable costs of prosecuting such appeal, protest, abatement, or other 
contest.  Large Load Interconnection Customer and Participating TO shall 
cooperate in good faith with respect to any such contest.  Unless the 
payment of such taxes is a prerequisite to an appeal or abatement or 
cannot be deferred, no amount shall be payable by Large Load 
Interconnection Customer to Participating TO for such taxes until they are 
assessed by a final, non-appealable order by any court or agency of 
competent jurisdiction.  In the event that a tax payment is withheld and 
ultimately due and payable after appeal, Large Load Interconnection 
Customer will be responsible for all taxes, interest and penalties, other 
than penalties attributable to any delay caused by Participating TO. 
 
5.12 Tax Status.  Each Party shall cooperate with the other to maintain the other 
Party’s tax status.  Nothing in this TSRA is intended to adversely affect any 
Participating TO’s tax exempt status with respect to the issuance of bonds.  
 
5.13 Modification. 
 
 
5.13.1 General.  Either Party may undertake modifications to its facilities.  If a 
Party plans to undertake a modification that reasonably may be expected 
to affect the other Party’s facilities, that Party shall provide to the other 
Party sufficient information regarding such modification so that the other 
Party may evaluate the potential impact of such modification prior to 
commencement of the work.  Such information shall be deemed to be 
confidential hereunder and shall include information concerning the timing 
of such modifications and whether such modifications are expected to 
interrupt the flow of electricity to the Large Load.  The Party desiring to 
perform such work shall provide the relevant drawings, plans, and 
specifications to the other Party at least ninety (90) Calendar Days in 
advance of the commencement of the work or such shorter period upon
--- PAGE 180 ---
Southern California Edison Company 
FERC Electric Tariff, Third Revised Volume No. 6 
 
which the Parties may agree, which agreement shall not unreasonably be 
withheld, conditioned or delayed. 
 
In the case of Large Load modifications that do not require Large Load 
Interconnection Customer to submit an Interconnection Request, the 
proposed modification shall be evaluated in accordance with LLIP Section 
4.6.1.6. If the proposed modification is determined not to be a Material 
Modification, the Participating TO shall provide, within forty-five (45) 
Calendar Days (or such other time as the Parties may agree), an estimate 
of any additional modifications to the Transmission System, Participating 
TO’s Interconnection Facilities, Distribution Upgrades, or Large Load 
Network Upgrades necessitated by such Large Load Interconnection 
Customer modification and a good faith estimate of the costs thereof. 
 
 
5.13.2 Standards.  Any additions, modifications, or replacements made to a 
Party’s facilities shall be designed, constructed and operated in 
accordance with this TSRA and Good Utility Practice. 
 
 
5.13.3 Modification Costs.  Large Load Interconnection Customer shall not be 
directly assigned for the costs of any additions, modifications, or 
replacements that Participating TO makes to Participating TO’s 
Interconnection Facilities or the Participating TO’s Transmission System to 
facilitate the interconnection of a third party to Participating TO’s 
Interconnection Facilities or the Participating TO’s Transmission System, 
or to provide transmission interconnection service to a third party under 
the TO Tariff.  Large Load Interconnection Customer shall be responsible 
for the costs of any additions, modifications, or replacements to Large 
Load Interconnection Customer’s Interconnection Facilities that may be 
necessary to maintain or upgrade such Large Load Interconnection 
Customer’s Interconnection Facilities consistent with Applicable Laws and 
Regulations, Applicable Reliability Standards or Good Utility Practice. 
 
Article 6. 
Testing and Inspection 
 
6.1 
Pre-Service Commencement Date Testing and Modifications.  Prior to the 
Service Commencement Date, Participating TO shall test Participating TO’s 
Interconnection Facilities, Distribution Upgrades, and Large Load Network 
Upgrades and Large Load Interconnection Customer shall test the Large Load 
and co-located generation (if applicable) and Large Load Interconnection 
Customer’s Interconnection Facilities to ensure their safe and reliable operation.  
Each Party shall make any modifications to its facilities that are found to be 
necessary as a result of such testing.  Large Load Interconnection Customer 
shall bear the cost of all such testing and modifications.  The Large Load 
Interconnection Customer shall not commence initial parallel operation of the 
Large Load and co-located generation (if applicable) with the Participating TO’s 
Transmission System until the Participating TO provides prior written approval as
--- PAGE 181 ---
Southern California Edison Company 
FERC Electric Tariff, Third Revised Volume No. 6 
 
set forth in Appendix C, which approval shall not be unreasonably withheld, for 
operation of such Large Load and co-located generation (if applicable).  Large 
Load Interconnection Customer shall receive test energy at the Large Load only 
if it has arranged for the receipt of such test energy. 
 
6.2 
Post-Service Commencement Operation Date Testing and Modifications.  
Each Party shall at its own expense perform routine inspection and testing of its 
facilities and equipment in accordance with Good Utility Practice as may be 
necessary to ensure the continued interconnection of the Large Load and co-
located generation (if applicable) with the Participating TO’s Transmission 
System in a safe and reliable manner.  Each Party shall have the right, upon 
advance written notice, to require reasonable additional testing of the other 
Party’s facilities, at the requesting Party’s expense, as may be in accordance 
with Good Utility Practice. 
 
6.3 
Right to Observe Testing.  Each Party shall notify the other Party in advance of 
its performance of tests of its Interconnection Facilities, the Large Load and co-
located generation (if applicable).  The other Party has the right, at its own 
expense, to observe such testing. 
 
6.4 
Right to Inspect.  Each Party shall have the right, but shall have no obligation 
to: (i) observe the other Party’s tests and/or inspection of any of its System 
Protection Facilities and other protective equipment; (ii) review the settings of the 
other Party’s System Protection Facilities and other protective equipment; and 
(iii) review the other Party’s maintenance records relative to the Interconnection 
Facilities, the System Protection Facilities and other protective equipment.  A 
Party may exercise these rights from time to time as it deems necessary upon 
reasonable notice to the other Party.  The exercise or non-exercise by a Party of 
any such rights shall not be construed as an endorsement or confirmation of any 
element or condition of the Interconnection Facilities or the System Protection 
Facilities or other protective equipment or the operation thereof, or as a warranty 
as to the fitness, safety, desirability, or reliability of same.  Any information that a 
Party obtains through the exercise of any of its rights under this Article 6.4 shall 
be deemed to be Confidential Information and treated pursuant to Article 22 of 
this TSRA. 
 
Article 7. 
Metering   
 
7.1 
General.  Each Party shall comply with any Applicable Reliability Standards and 
the Electric Reliability Organization requirements.  The Large Load 
Interconnection Customer shall comply with the provisions of the ISO Tariff 
regarding metering, including Section 10 of the ISO Tariff.  Unless otherwise 
agreed by the Parties, Participating TO may install additional Metering 
Equipment at the Point of Interconnection prior to any operation of the Large 
Load and shall own, operate, test and maintain such Metering Equipment.  
Power flows to the Large Load shall be measured at or, at Participating TO’s
--- PAGE 182 ---
Southern California Edison Company 
FERC Electric Tariff, Third Revised Volume No. 6 
 
option, compensated to, the Point of Interconnection.  Large Load 
Interconnection Customer’s access to meter data shall be provided in 
accordance with the ISO Tariff.  Large Load Interconnection Customer shall bear 
all reasonable documented costs associated with the purchase, installation, 
operation, testing and maintenance of the Metering Equipment. 
 
7.2 
Check Meters.  Large Load Interconnection Customer, at its option and 
expense, may install and operate, on its premises and on its side of the Point of 
Interconnection, one or more check meters to check the ISO-polled meters or 
Participating TO’s meters.  Such check meters shall be for check purposes only 
and shall not be used for the measurement of power flows for purposes of this 
TSRA, except in the case that no other means are available on a temporary 
basis at the option of the Participating TO.  The check meters shall be subject at 
all reasonable times to inspection and examination by Participating TO or its 
designee.  The installation, operation and maintenance thereof shall be 
performed entirely by Large Load Interconnection Customer in accordance with 
Good Utility Practice. 
 
7.3 
Participating TO Retail Metering.  If the Large Load is a retail transmission 
customer of the Participating TO, the Participating TO shall install retail revenue 
quality meters and associated equipment, pursuant to the Participating TO’s 
applicable retail tariffs. 
 
Article 8. 
Communications 
 
8.1 
Large Load Interconnection Customer Obligations.  Large Load 
Interconnection Customer shall maintain satisfactory operating communications 
with Participating TO’s Transmission System dispatcher or representative 
designated by Participating TO.  Large Load Interconnection Customer shall 
provide standard voice line and dedicated voice line at its control room or central 
dispatch facility through use of either the public telephone system, or a voice 
communications system that does not rely on the public telephone system.  
Large Load Interconnection Customer shall also provide the dedicated data 
circuit(s) necessary to provide Large Load Interconnection Customer data to 
Participating TO as set forth in Appendix F, Security Arrangements Details.  The 
data circuit(s) shall extend from the Large Load to the location(s) specified by 
Participating TO.  Any required maintenance of such communications equipment 
shall be performed by Large Load Interconnection Customer.  Operational 
communications shall be activated and maintained under, but not be limited to, 
the following events: system paralleling or separation, scheduled and 
unscheduled shutdowns, equipment clearances, and hourly and daily load data. 
 
8.2 
Remote Terminal Unit.  Prior to the Service Commencement Date of the Large 
Load, a Remote Terminal Unit, or equivalent data collection and transfer 
equipment acceptable to the Parties, shall be installed by Large Load 
Interconnection Customer, or by Participating TO at Large Load Interconnection
--- PAGE 183 ---
Southern California Edison Company 
FERC Electric Tariff, Third Revised Volume No. 6 
 
Customer’s expense, to gather accumulated and instantaneous data to be 
telemetered to the location(s) designated by Participating TO through use of a 
dedicated point-to-point data circuit(s) as indicated in Article 8.1.  The 
communication protocol for the data circuit(s) shall be specified by Participating 
TO.  Instantaneous bi-directional analog real power and reactive power flow 
information must be telemetered directly to the location(s) specified by 
Participating TO. The telemetered data shall provide, but not be limited to, 
operational status and real-time consumption to the ISO and Participating TO in 
accordance with the ISO’s standards for direct telemetry and consistent with the 
requirement for telemetry set forth in Section 7.6.1 of the ISO Tariff.   
 
Each Party will promptly advise the other Party if it detects or otherwise learns of 
any metering, telemetry or communications equipment errors or malfunctions that 
require the attention and/or correction by the other Party.  The Party owning such 
equipment shall correct such error or malfunction as soon as reasonably feasible.  
 
8.3 
No Annexation.  Any and all equipment placed on the premises of a Party shall 
be and remain the property of the Party providing such equipment regardless of 
the mode and manner of annexation or attachment to real property, unless 
otherwise mutually agreed by the Parties. 
 
Article 9. 
Operations 
 
9.1 
General.  Each Party shall comply with Applicable Reliability Standards and the 
Electric Reliability Organization requirements.  Each Party shall provide to the 
other Party all information that may reasonably be required by the other Party to 
comply with Applicable Laws and Regulations and Applicable Reliability 
Standards. 
 
9.2 
Participating TO Obligations. The Participating TO and ISO shall cause the 
Participating TO’s Transmission System to be operated and controlled in a safe 
and reliable manner and in accordance with this TSRA.  The Participating TO at 
the Large Load Interconnection Customer’s expense shall cause the Participating 
TO’s Interconnection Facilities to be operated, maintained and controlled in a 
safe and reliable manner and in accordance with this TSRA.  The Participating 
TO and ISO may provide operating instructions to the Large Load 
Interconnection Customer consistent with this TSRA and Participating TO and 
ISO operating protocols and procedures as they may change from time to 
time.  The Participating TO and ISO will consider changes to their operating 
protocols and procedures proposed by the Large Load Interconnection 
Customer. 
  
9.3 
Large Load Interconnection Customer Obligations.  The Large Load 
Interconnection Customer shall at its own expense operate, maintain and control 
the Large Load, co-located generation (if applicable), and the Large Load 
Interconnection Customer’s Interconnection Facilities, in a safe and reliable
--- PAGE 184 ---
Southern California Edison Company 
FERC Electric Tariff, Third Revised Volume No. 6 
 
manner and in accordance with this TSRA.  The Large Load Interconnection 
Customer shall operate the Large Load, co-located generation (if applicable), 
and the Large Load Interconnection Customer’s Interconnection Facilities, in 
accordance with all applicable requirements of the Balancing Authority Area of 
which it is part, including such requirements as set forth in Appendix D of 
this TSRA.  Appendix D will be modified to reflect changes to the requirements 
as they may change from time to time.  A Party may request that another Party 
provide copies of the requirements set forth in Appendix D of this TSRA.  The 
Large Load Interconnection Customer shall not commence operation of the 
Large Load or co-located generation (if applicable) in parallel with the 
Participating TO’s Transmission System until the Participating TO provides prior 
written approval, which approval shall not be unreasonably withheld, for 
operation of the Large Load or co-located generation (if applicable). 
 
9.4 
Start-Up and Synchronization.  Consistent with the Parties’ mutually 
acceptable procedures, Large Load Interconnection Customer is responsible for 
the proper synchronization of the Large Load and co-located generation (if 
applicable) to Participating TO’s Transmission System. 
 
9.5 
Reactive Power. 
 
. 
Power Factor Design Criteria.   
Large Load Interconnection Customer shall design the Large Load to 
maintain a composite power flow at POI, at a power factor within the range 
of 0.99 leading to 0.97 lagging. This power factor range standard shall be 
dynamic and can be met using, for example, power electronics designed 
to supply this level of reactive capability (taking into account any 
limitations due to voltage level, real power output, etc.) or fixed and 
switched capacitors, or a combination of the two. 
 
  
9.6 
Outages and Performance Requirements. 
 
 
9.6.1 Outage Coordination.  Large Load Interconnection Customer shall 
coordinate with Participating TO and provide reasonable notice of any 
outages or maintenance on Large Load Interconnection Customer’s 
Interconnection Facilities consistent with Good Utility Practice.   
 
 
9.6.2 Ride Through Capability and Performance.  The Participating TO’s 
Transmission System is designed to automatically activate a load-shed 
program as required by the Electric Reliability Organization in the event of 
an under-frequency system disturbance.  Large Load Interconnection 
Customer shall implement under-frequency and over-frequency relay set 
points, or equivalent electronic controls, for the Large Load as required by 
the Electric Reliability Organization to ensure frequency “ride through” 
capability of the ISO Controlled Grid.  Large Load response to frequency 
deviations of pre-determined magnitudes, both under-frequency and over-
--- PAGE 185 ---
Southern California Edison Company 
FERC Electric Tariff, Third Revised Volume No. 6 
 
frequency deviations, shall be studied and coordinated with Transmission 
Provider in accordance with Good Utility Practice, the latest applicable 
reliability standards, and the Participating TO’s Interconnection Handbook.  
Large Load Interconnection Customer shall also implement under-voltage 
and over-voltage relay set points, or equivalent electronic controls, as 
required by the Electric Reliability Organization to ensure voltage “ride 
through” capability of the Participating TO’s Transmission System in 
accordance with Good Utility Practice, the latest applicable reliability 
standards, and the Participating TO’s Interconnection Handbook.  The 
term “ride through” as used herein shall mean the ability of a Large Load 
to stay connected to and synchronized with the ISO Controlled Grid during 
system disturbances within a range of under-frequency, over-frequency, 
under-voltage, and over-voltage conditions, in accordance with Good 
Utility Practice and consistent with any standards and guidelines that are 
applied to other Generating Facilities in the Balancing Authority Area on a 
comparable basis.  The Large Load must ensure “ride through” capability 
for abnormal frequency and voltage conditions within the “no trip zone” 
defined by the Participating TO’s Interconnection Handbook, subject to its 
physical limitations.   
 
 
9.6.3 System Protection and Other Control Requirements. 
 
 
 
9.6.3.1 
System Protection Facilities.  Large Load Interconnection 
Customer shall, at its expense, install, operate and maintain 
System Protection Facilities as a part of the Large Load, and co-
located generation, if applicable, or Large Load Interconnection 
Customer’s Interconnection Facilities.  Participating TO shall install 
at Large Load Interconnection Customer’s expense any System 
Protection Facilities that may be required on Participating TO’s 
Interconnection Facilities or the Participating TO’s Transmission 
System as a result of the interconnection of the Large Load and 
Large Load Interconnection Customer’s Interconnection Facilities. 
 
 
 
9.6.3.2 Each Party’s protection facilities shall be designed and coordinated 
with other systems in accordance with Applicable Reliability 
Standards, Electric Reliability Organization criteria, and Good Utility 
Practice. 
 
 
 
9.6.3.3 Each Party shall be responsible for protection of its facilities 
consistent with Good Utility Practice. 
 
 
 
9.6.3.4 Each Party’s protective relay design shall incorporate the 
necessary test switches to perform the tests required in Article 6.  
The required test switches will be placed such that they allow 
operation of lockout relays while preventing breaker failure
--- PAGE 186 ---
Southern California Edison Company 
FERC Electric Tariff, Third Revised Volume No. 6 
 
schemes from operating and causing unnecessary breaker 
operations and/or the tripping of the Large Load. 
 
 
 
9.6.3.5  
Each Party will test, operate and maintain System Protection 
Facilities in accordance with Good Utility Practice and, if applicable, 
the requirements of the Participating TO’s Interconnection 
Handbook. 
 
 
 
9.6.3.6 
Prior to the Service Commencement Date, each Party or its 
agent shall perform a complete calibration test and functional trip 
test of the System Protection Facilities.  At intervals suggested by 
Good Utility Practice, the standards and procedures of the 
Participating TO, including, if applicable, the requirements of the 
Participating TO’s Interconnection Handbook, and following any 
apparent malfunction of the System Protection Facilities, each 
Party shall perform both calibration and functional trip tests of its 
System Protection Facilities.  These tests do not require the tripping 
of the Large Load.  These tests do, however, require that all 
protective relays and lockout contacts be activated. 
 
 
9.6.4 Requirements for Protection.  In compliance with Good Utility Practice 
and, if applicable, the requirements of the Participating TO’s 
Interconnection Handbook, Large Load Interconnection Customer shall 
provide, install, own, and maintain relays, circuit breakers and all other 
devices necessary to remove any fault contribution of the Large Load, or 
co-located generation, if applicable, to any short circuit occurring on the 
Participating TO’s Transmission System not otherwise isolated by 
Participating TO’s equipment, such that the removal of the fault 
contribution shall be coordinated with the protective requirements of the 
Participating TO’s Transmission System.  Such protective equipment shall 
include, without limitation, a disconnecting device or switch with load-
interrupting capability located between the Large Load, and co-located 
generation, if applicable, and the Participating TO’s Transmission System 
at a site selected upon mutual agreement (not to be unreasonably 
withheld, conditioned or delayed) of the Parties.  Large Load 
Interconnection Customer shall be responsible for protection of the Large 
Load, and co-located generation, if applicable, and Large Load 
Interconnection Customer’s other equipment from such conditions as 
negative sequence currents, over- or under-frequency, sudden load 
rejection, over- or under-voltage, and generator loss-of-field.  Large Load 
Interconnection Customer shall be solely responsible to disconnect the 
Large Load, or co-located generation, if applicable, and Large Load 
Interconnection Customer’s other equipment if conditions on the 
Participating TO’s Transmission System could adversely affect the Large 
Load, or co-located generation, if applicable.
--- PAGE 187 ---
Southern California Edison Company 
FERC Electric Tariff, Third Revised Volume No. 6 
 
 
9.6.5 Power Quality.  Neither Party’s facilities shall cause excessive voltage 
flicker nor introduce excessive distortion to the sinusoidal voltage or 
current waves as defined by the latest applicable standards identified in 
the Participating TO’s Interconnection Handbook.  In the event of a conflict 
among these standards, the alternative Applicable Reliability Standard or 
Electrical Reliability Organization standard shall control. 
 
9.7 
Operating Limitations Related to FILI. The conditions and limitations 
applicable to Flexible Interim Load Interconnection (FILI) service, including 
curtailments, shall be specified in Appendix E to this TSRA. 
 
9.8 
Switching and Tagging Rules.  Each Party shall provide the other Party a copy 
of its switching and tagging rules that are applicable to the other Party’s activities.  
Such switching and tagging rules shall be developed on a non-discriminatory 
basis.  The Parties shall comply with applicable switching and tagging rules, as 
amended from time to time, in obtaining clearances for work or for switching 
operations on equipment. 
 
9.9 
Disturbance Analysis Data Exchange.  The Parties will cooperate with one 
another in the analysis of disturbances to either the Large Load or Participating 
TO’s Transmission System by gathering and providing access to any information 
relating to any disturbance, including information from oscillography, protective 
relay targets, breaker operations and sequence of events records, and any 
disturbance information required by Good Utility Practice and Applicable 
Reliability Standards. 
 
9.10 Compliance with ISO Tariff on Large Load Interconnections.  Each Large 
Load must comply, as applicable, with the ISO Tariff provisions in ISO Tariff Section 
25.7, and any complementary technical specifications contained in SCE's 
Interconnection Handbook. 
 
Article 10. 
Maintenance 
 
10.1 Participating TO Obligations.  Participating TO shall maintain the Participating 
TO’s Transmission System and Participating TO’s Interconnection Facilities in a 
safe and reliable manner and in accordance with this TSRA. 
 
10.2 Large Load Interconnection Customer Obligations.  Large Load 
Interconnection Customer shall maintain the Large Load and co-located 
generation, if applicable, and Large Load Interconnection Customer’s 
Interconnection Facilities in a safe and reliable manner and in accordance with 
this TSRA. 
 
10.3 Coordination.  The Parties shall confer regularly to coordinate the planning, 
scheduling and performance of preventive and corrective maintenance on the
--- PAGE 188 ---
Southern California Edison Company 
FERC Electric Tariff, Third Revised Volume No. 6 
 
Large Load, and co-located generation, if applicable, and the Interconnection 
Facilities. 
 
10.4 Secondary Systems.  Each Party shall cooperate with the other in the 
inspection, maintenance, and testing of control or power circuits that operate 
below 600 volts, AC or DC, including, but not limited to, any hardware, control or 
protective devices, cables, conductors, electric raceways, secondary equipment 
panels, transducers, batteries, chargers, and voltage and current transformers 
that directly affect the operation of a Party’s facilities and equipment which may 
reasonably be expected to impact the other Party.  Each Party shall provide 
advance notice to the other Party before undertaking any work on such circuits, 
especially on electrical circuits involving circuit breaker trip and close contacts, 
current transformers, or potential transformers. 
 
10.5 Operating and Maintenance Expenses.  Subject to the provisions herein 
addressing the use of facilities by others, and except for operations and 
maintenance expenses associated with modifications made for providing 
interconnection or transmission service to a third party and such third party pays 
for such expenses, Large Load Interconnection Customer shall be responsible 
for all reasonable expenses including overheads, associated with: (1) owning, 
operating, maintaining, repairing, and replacing Large Load Interconnection 
Customer’s Interconnection Facilities; and (2) operation, maintenance, repair and 
replacement of Participating TO’s Interconnection Facilities and Distribution 
Upgrades. 
 
Article 11. 
Performance Obligation 
 
11.1 Large Load Interconnection Customer Interconnection Facilities.  Large 
Load Interconnection Customer shall design, procure, construct, install, own 
and/or control Large Load Interconnection Customer Interconnection Facilities 
described in Appendix A at its sole expense. 
 
11.2 Participating TO’s Interconnection Facilities and Distribution Upgrades.  
Participating TO shall design, procure, construct, install, own and/or control the 
Participating TO’s Interconnection Facilities and Distribution Upgrades described 
in Appendix A at the sole expense of the Large Load Interconnection Customer.  
The Large Load Interconnection Customer shall be responsible for funding all 
costs related to Participating TO’s Interconnection Facilities and Distribution 
Upgrades.  The costs set forth in Appendix A are estimates only and will not 
establish any cap or maximum cost responsibility limit on the cost responsibility 
of the Large Load Interconnection Customer for the Participating TO’s 
Interconnection Facilities and Distribution Upgrades.  The Large Load 
Interconnection Customer shall be responsible for the actual costs related to 
Participating TO’s Interconnection Facilities and Distribution Upgrades.  This 
Section 11.2 does not apply to Distribution Upgrades required by Large Load 
Interconnection Customers who are retail transmission customers of participating
--- PAGE 189 ---
Southern California Edison Company 
FERC Electric Tariff, Third Revised Volume No. 6 
 
TO, as such facilities and their costs are governed by the Local Regulatory 
Authority. 
 
11.3 Large Load Network Upgrades.  Participating TO shall design, procure, 
construct, install, and own the Large Load Network Upgrades described in 
Appendix A. The Participating TO shall be responsible for securing the costs of 
the Large Load Network Upgrades attributable to the Large Load subject to the 
financial security and payment obligations specified in Appendix B. The costs set 
forth in Appendix A are estimates only. 
 
11.3.1    Non-Export Co-Located Generation Large Load Network Upgrades. 
 
Participating TO shall design, procure, construct, install, own and/or 
control the Large Load Network Upgrades attributable to non-export co-
located generation, as described in Appendix A, at the sole expense of the 
Large Load Interconnection Customer. The Large Load Interconnection 
Customer shall be responsible for funding all costs related to such Large 
Load Network Upgrades.  The costs set forth in Appendix A are estimates 
only and will not establish any cap or maximum cost responsibility limit on 
the cost responsibility of the Large Load Interconnection Customer for the 
Large Load Network Upgrades. The Large Load Interconnection Customer 
shall be responsible for the actual costs related to Large Load Network 
Upgrades. When the Large Load Interconnection Customer is the 
Participating TO’s retail transmission customer, the cost assignment and 
responsibility for Large Load Network Upgrades associated with non-
export co-located generation will be made in accordance with the 
Participating TO’s retail tariffs subject to the Local Regulatory Authority. 
 
11.4 Provision of Interconnection Financial Security.   
 
 
11.4.1 Participating TO’s Interconnection Facilities and Distribution 
Upgrades. At least thirty (30) Calendar Days prior to the commencement of the 
engineering, design, procurement, installation, or construction of the Participating 
TO’s Interconnection Facilities or Distribution Upgrades, Large Load 
Interconnection Customer shall provide Participating TO, at Large Load 
Interconnection Customer’s option, a guarantee, a surety bond, letter of credit or 
other form of security that is reasonably acceptable to Participating TO, in 
accordance with LLIP Section 4.7.1.  Such security for payment, as specified in 
Appendix A of this TSRA, shall be in an amount sufficient to cover the costs for 
constructing, procuring and installing the applicable portion of Participating TO’s 
Interconnection Facilities and Distribution Upgrades and shall be reduced on a 
dollar-for-dollar basis for payments made to Participating TO for these purposes.  
Participating TO must specify, in Appendix C of this TSRA, the dates for which 
Interconnection Customer must provide additional security for construction of 
each discrete portion of Participating TO’s Interconnection Facilities and 
Distribution Upgrades and Large Load Interconnection Customer must provide 
such additional security.    This Section 11.4.1 does not apply to Distribution
--- PAGE 190 ---
Southern California Edison Company 
FERC Electric Tariff, Third Revised Volume No. 6 
 
Upgrades required by Large Load Interconnection Customers who are retail 
transmission customers of participating TO, as such facilities and their costs are 
governed by the Local Regulatory Authority. 
 
11.4.2 Security Requirements.   
 
11.4.2.1 Large Load Network Upgrades and Minimum Annual 
Revenue Contribution. At least thirty (30) Calendar Days prior to 
the commencement of the engineering, design, procurement, 
installation, or construction of the Large Load Network Upgrades 
attributable to the Large Load, the Large Load Interconnection 
Customer shall provide Interconnection Financial Security to 
mitigate potential cost shifting to other users of the ISO Controlled 
Grid. Such additional security requirements, associated payment 
obligations, and the methodology utilized for calculating such 
requirements are specified in Appendix B to this TSRA.  
 
11.4.2.2 Non-Export Co-Located Generation Large Load Network 
Upgrades. At least thirty (30) Calendar Days prior to the 
commencement of the engineering, design, procurement, 
installation, or construction of the Large Load Network Upgrades 
attributable to non-export co-located generation, Large Load 
Interconnection Customer shall provide Participating TO, at Large 
Load Interconnection Customer’s option, a guarantee, a surety 
bond, letter of credit or other form of security that is reasonably 
acceptable to Participating TO, in accordance with LLIP Section 
4.7.1.  Such financial security shall be in an amount as specified 
by the Participating TO in Appendix A of this TSRA and provided 
by the Large Load Interconnection Customer by the date(s) set 
forth in Appendix C. When the Large Load Interconnection 
Customer is the Participating TO’s retail transmission customer, 
the security for Large Load Network Upgrades associated with 
non-export co-located generation will be made in accordance with 
the Participating TO’s retail tariffs subject to the Local Regulatory 
Authority. 
 
        11.4.2.3 ISO-Identified Network Upgrades. At least thirty (30) Calendar 
Days prior to the commencement of the engineering, design, 
procurement, installation, or construction of the ISO-Identified 
Network Upgrades attributable to the Large Load, the Large Load 
Interconnection Customer shall provide financial security to 
mitigate shifting of the cost of the ISO-Identified Network 
Upgrades to other users of the ISO Controlled Grid. Such 
additional security requirements, associated payment obligations, 
and the methodology utilized for calculating such requirements 
are specified in Appendix B to this TSRA.
