11.4.2.4 Large Load Interconnection Customer may avoid duplicative 
Interconnection Financial Security requirements by demonstrating 
to Participating TO’s satisfaction that security or payments 
provided pursuant to a retail agreement with the Large Load 
Owner satisfies some portion of Large Load Interconnection 
Customer’s obligations under Articles 11.4.2.1, 11.4.2.2, and 
11.4.2.3.   
 
Article 12. 
Invoice 
 
12.1 General.  The Participating TO shall submit to the Large Load Interconnection 
Customer, on a monthly basis, invoices of amounts due for the preceding month.  
Each invoice shall state the month to which the invoice applies and fully describe 
the services and equipment provided.   
 
12.2 
Final Invoice.  Within twelve (12) months after completion of the construction of 
Participating TO’s Interconnection Facilities and Distribution Upgrades, 
Participating TO shall provide an invoice of the final cost of the construction of 
Participating TO’s Interconnection Facilities and Distribution Upgrades, and shall 
set forth such costs in sufficient detail to enable Large Load Interconnection 
Customer to compare the actual costs with the estimates and to ascertain 
deviations, if any, from the cost estimates.  Participating TO shall refund to Large 
Load Interconnection Customer any amount by which the actual payment by 
Large Load Interconnection Customer for estimated costs exceeds the actual 
costs of construction within thirty (30) Calendar Days of the issuance of such final 
construction invoice. 
 
12.3 Payment.  Invoices shall be rendered to the paying Party at the address 
specified in Appendix G.  The Party receiving the invoice shall pay the invoice 
within thirty (30) Calendar Days of receipt.  All payments shall be made in 
immediately available funds payable to the other Party, or by wire transfer to a 
bank named and account designated by the invoicing Party.  Payment of invoices 
by either Party will not constitute a waiver of any rights or claims either Party may 
have under this TSRA. 
 
12.4 Disputes.  In the event of a billing dispute between Participating TO and Large 
Load Interconnection Customer, Participating TO shall continue to provide 
Transmission Interconnection Service under this TSRA as long as Large Load 
Interconnection Customer: (i) continues to make all payments not in dispute; and 
(ii) pays to Participating TO or into an independent escrow account the portion of 
the invoice in dispute, pending resolution of such dispute.  If Large Load 
Interconnection Customer fails to meet these two requirements for continuation 
of service, then Participating TO may provide notice to Large Load 
Interconnection Customer of a Default pursuant to Article 17.  Within thirty (30) 
Calendar Days after the resolution of the dispute, the Party that owes money to
--- PAGE 192 ---
Southern California Edison Company 
FERC Electric Tariff, Third Revised Volume No. 6 
 
the other Party shall pay the amount due with interest calculated in accord with 
the methodology set forth in FERC’s regulations at 18 CFR § 35.19a(a)(2)(iii). 
 
Article 13. 
Emergencies 
 
13.1 Definition.  "Emergency Condition" shall mean a condition or situation: (1) that in 
the judgment of the Party making the claim is imminently likely to endanger life or 
property; or (2) that, in the case of the ISO, is imminently likely (as determined in 
a non-discriminatory manner) to cause a material adverse effect on the security 
of, or damage to, the ISO Controlled Grid or the electric systems of others to 
which the ISO Controlled Grid is directly connected; (3) that, in the case of the 
Participating TO, is imminently likely (as determined in a non-discriminatory 
manner) to cause a material adverse effect on the security of, or damage to, the 
Participating TO’s Transmission System, Participating TO’s Interconnection 
Facilities, Distribution System, or the electric systems of others to which the 
Participating TO’s electric system is directly connected; or (4) that, in the case of 
the Large Load Interconnection Customer, is imminently likely (as determined in 
a non-discriminatory manner) to cause a material adverse effect on the security 
of, or damage to, the Large Load or Large Load Interconnection Customer’s 
Interconnection Facilities.   
 
13.2 Obligations.  Each Party shall comply with the Emergency Condition procedures 
of the ISO, Electric Reliability Organization, Applicable Reliability Standards, 
Applicable Laws and Regulations, and any emergency procedures set forth in 
this TSRA. 
 
13.3 
Notice.  Participating TO shall notify Large Load Interconnection Customer 
promptly when it becomes aware of an Emergency Condition that affects 
Participating TO’s Interconnection Facilities, Distribution System or the ISO 
Controlled Grid that may reasonably be expected to affect Large Load 
Interconnection Customer’s operation of the Large Load or Large Load 
Interconnection Customer’s Interconnection Facilities.  Large Load 
Interconnection Customer shall notify Participating TO promptly when it becomes 
aware of an Emergency Condition that affects the Large Load or Large Load 
Interconnection Customer’s Interconnection Facilities that may reasonably be 
expected to affect the Distribution System, ISO Controlled Grid or Participating 
TO’s Interconnection Facilities.  To the extent information is known, the 
notification shall describe the Emergency Condition, the extent of the damage or 
deficiency, the expected effect on the operation of Large Load Interconnection 
Customer’s or Participating TO’s facilities and operations, its anticipated duration 
and the corrective action taken and/or to be taken.  The initial notice shall be 
followed as soon as practicable with written notice. 
 
13.4 
Immediate Action.  Unless, in Large Load Interconnection Customer’s 
reasonable judgment, immediate action is required, Large Load Interconnection 
Customer shall obtain the consent of Participating TO, such consent to not be
--- PAGE 193 ---
Southern California Edison Company 
FERC Electric Tariff, Third Revised Volume No. 6 
 
unreasonably withheld, prior to performing any manual switching operations at 
the Large Load or Large Load Interconnection Customer’s Interconnection 
Facilities in response to an Emergency Condition either declared by Participating 
TO or ISO or in response to any other emergency condition. 
 
13.5 Participating TO Authority. 
 
 
13.5.1 General.  Participating TO may take whatever actions or inactions with 
regard to the Distribution System and Participating TO’s Transmission 
System or Participating TO’s Interconnection Facilities it deems necessary 
during an Emergency Condition in order to (i) preserve public health and 
safety, (ii) preserve the reliability of the Distribution System and 
Participating TO’s Transmission System or Participating TO’s 
Interconnection Facilities, (iii) limit or prevent damage, and (iv) expedite 
restoration of service. 
 
Participating TO shall use Reasonable Efforts to minimize the effect of 
such actions or inactions on the Large Load or Large Load Interconnection 
Customer’s Interconnection Facilities.  Participating TO may, on the basis 
of technical considerations, require the Large Load to mitigate an 
Emergency Condition by taking actions necessary and limited in scope to 
remedy the Emergency Condition, including, but not limited to, directing 
Large Load Interconnection Customer to shut-down the Large Load; 
implementing a reduction or disconnection pursuant to Article 13.5.2.  
Large Load Interconnection Customer shall comply with all of Participating 
TO’s operating instructions concerning Large Load within the 
manufacturer’s design limitations of the Large Load’s equipment that is in 
service and physically available for operation at the time, in compliance 
with Applicable Laws and Regulations. 
 
 
13.5.2 Reduction and Disconnection.  Participating TO may reduce 
Transmission Interconnection Service or disconnect the Large Load or 
Large Load Interconnection Customer’s Interconnection Facilities, when 
such, reduction or disconnection is necessary under Good Utility Practice 
due to Emergency Conditions.  These rights are separate and distinct from 
any right of curtailment of the ISO pursuant to the ISO Tariff.  When 
Participating TO can schedule the reduction or disconnection in advance, 
Participating TO shall notify Large Load Interconnection Customer of the 
reasons, timing and expected duration of the reduction or disconnection.  
Participating TO shall coordinate with Large Load Interconnection 
Customer using Good Utility Practice to schedule the reduction or 
disconnection during periods of least impact to Large Load 
Interconnection Customer and Participating TO.  Any reduction or 
disconnection shall continue only for so long as reasonably necessary 
under Good Utility Practice.  The Parties shall cooperate with each other 
to restore the Large Load, the Interconnection Facilities, and the
--- PAGE 194 ---
Southern California Edison Company 
FERC Electric Tariff, Third Revised Volume No. 6 
 
Participating TO’s Transmission System to their normal operating state as 
soon as practicable consistent with Good Utility Practice. 
 
13.6 
Large Load Interconnection Customer Authority.  Consistent with Good Utility 
Practice and the TSRA and the LLIP, Large Load Interconnection Customer may 
take actions or inactions with regard to the Large Load or Large Load 
Interconnection Customer’s Interconnection Facilities during an Emergency 
Condition in order to (i) preserve public health and safety, (ii) preserve the 
reliability of the Large Load or Large Load Interconnection Customer’s 
Interconnection Facilities, (iii) limit or prevent damage, and (iv) expedite 
restoration of service.  Large Load Interconnection Customer shall use 
Reasonable Efforts to minimize the effect of such actions or inactions on the 
Participating TO’s Transmission System and Participating TO’s Interconnection 
facilities.  Participating TO shall use Reasonable Efforts to assist Large Load 
Interconnection Customer in such actions. 
 
13.7 Limited Liability.  Neither Party shall be liable to the other for any action it takes 
in responding to an Emergency Condition so long as such action is made in good 
faith and is consistent with Good Utility Practice. 
 
Article 14. 
Regulatory Requirements and Governing Law 
 
14.1 Regulatory Requirements.  Each Party’s obligations under this TSRA shall be 
subject to its receipt of any required approval or certificate from one or more 
Governmental Authorities in the form and substance satisfactory to the applying 
Party, or the Party making any required filings with, or providing notice to, such 
Governmental Authorities, and the expiration of any time period associated 
therewith.  Each Party shall in good faith seek and use its Reasonable Efforts to 
obtain such other approvals.   
 
14.2 Governing Law. 
 
14.2.1 The validity, interpretation and performance of this TSRA and each of its 
provisions shall be governed by the laws of the state where the Point of 
Interconnection is located, without regard to its conflicts of law principles. 
 
14.2.2 This TSRA is subject to all Applicable Laws and Regulations. 
 
14.2.3 Each Party expressly reserves the right to seek changes in, appeal, or 
otherwise contest any laws, orders, rules, or regulations of a 
Governmental Authority. 
 
Article 15. 
Notices. 
 
15.1 General.  Unless otherwise provided in this TSRA, any notice, demand or 
request required or permitted to be given by either Party to the other and any
--- PAGE 195 ---
Southern California Edison Company 
FERC Electric Tariff, Third Revised Volume No. 6 
 
instrument required or permitted to be tendered or delivered by either Party in 
writing to the other shall be effective when delivered and may be so given, 
tendered or delivered, by recognized national courier, or by depositing the same 
with the United States Postal Service with postage prepaid, for delivery by 
certified or registered mail, addressed to the Party, or personally delivered to the 
Party, at the address set out in Appendix G. 
 
Either Party may change the notice information in this TSRA by giving five (5) 
Business Days written notice prior to the effective date of the change. 
 
15.2 Billings and Payments.  Billings and payments shall be sent to the addresses 
set out in Appendix G. 
 
15.3 Alternative Forms of Notice.  Any notice or request required or permitted to be 
given by a Party to the other and not required by this Agreement to be given in 
writing may be so given by telephone or email to the telephone numbers and 
email addresses set out in Appendix G. 
 
15.4 Operations and Maintenance Notice.  Each Party shall notify the other Party in 
writing of the identity of the person(s) that it designates as the point(s) of contact 
with respect to the implementation of Articles 9 and 10. 
 
Article 16. 
Uncontrollable Force 
 
16.1 Uncontrollable Force. 
 
 
16.1.1 Economic hardship is not considered an Uncontrollable Force event. 
 
16.1.2 Neither Party shall be considered to be in Default with respect to any 
obligation hereunder, including obligations under Article 4, other than the 
obligation to pay money when due, if prevented from fulfilling such 
obligation by Uncontrollable Force.  A Party unable to fulfill any obligation 
hereunder (other than an obligation to pay money when due) by reason of 
an Uncontrollable Force shall give notice and the full particulars of such 
Uncontrollable Force to the other Party in writing or by telephone as soon 
as reasonably possible after the occurrence of the cause relied upon.  
Telephone notices given pursuant to this Article shall be confirmed in 
writing as soon as reasonably possible and shall specifically state full 
particulars of the Uncontrollable Force, the time and date when the 
Uncontrollable Force occurred and when the Uncontrollable Force is 
reasonably expected to cease.  The Party affected shall exercise due 
diligence to remove such disability with reasonable dispatch, but shall not 
be required to accede or agree to any provision not satisfactory to it in 
order to settle and terminate a strike or other labor disturbance. 
 
Article 17. 
Default
--- PAGE 196 ---
Southern California Edison Company 
FERC Electric Tariff, Third Revised Volume No. 6 
 
 
17.1 Default  
 
17.1.1 General.  No Default shall exist where such failure to discharge an 
obligation (other than the payment of money) is the result of an 
Uncontrollable Force as defined in this TSRA or the result of an act or 
omission of the other Party.  Upon a Breach, the non-breaching Party shall 
give written notice of such Breach to the breaching Party.  Except as 
provided in Article 17.1.2, the breaching Party shall have thirty (30) 
Calendar Days from receipt of the Default notice within which to cure such 
Breach; provided however, if such Breach is not capable of cure within 
thirty (30) Calendar Days, the breaching Party shall commence such cure 
within thirty (30) Calendar Days after notice and continuously and 
diligently complete such cure within ninety (90) Calendar Days from 
receipt of the Default notice; and, if cured within such time, the Breach 
specified in such notice shall cease to exist. 
 
17.1.2 Right to Terminate.  If a Breach is not cured as provided in this Article, or 
if a Breach is not capable of being cured within the period provided for 
herein, the non-breaching Party shall have the right to declare a Default 
and terminate this TSRA by written notice at any time until cure occurs, 
and be relieved of any further obligation hereunder and, whether or not 
that Party terminates this TSRA, to recover from the breaching Party all 
amounts due hereunder, plus all other damages and remedies to which it 
is entitled at law or in equity.  The provisions of this Article will survive 
termination of this TSRA. 
 
Article 18. 
Indemnity, Consequential Damages and Insurance 
 
18.1 Indemnity.  The Parties shall at all times indemnify, defend, and hold the other 
Party harmless from, any and all damages, losses, claims, including claims and 
actions relating to injury to or death of any person or damage to property, 
demand, suits, recoveries, costs and expenses, court costs, attorney fees, and 
all other obligations by or to third parties, arising out of or resulting from the other 
Party’s action or inactions of its obligations under this TSRA on behalf of the 
Indemnifying Party, except in cases of gross negligence or intentional 
wrongdoing by the Indemnified Party. 
 
18.1.1 Indemnified Person.  If an Indemnified Person is entitled to 
indemnification under this Article 18 as a result of a claim by a third party, 
and the Indemnifying Party fails, after notice and reasonable opportunity to 
proceed under Article 18.1, to assume the defense of such claim, such 
Indemnified Person may at the expense of the Indemnifying Party contest, 
settle or consent to the entry of any judgment with respect to, or pay in full, 
such claim.
--- PAGE 197 ---
Southern California Edison Company 
FERC Electric Tariff, Third Revised Volume No. 6 
 
18.1.2 Indemnifying Party.  If an Indemnifying Party is obligated to indemnify 
and hold any Indemnified Person harmless under this Article 18, the 
amount owing to the Indemnified Person shall be the amount of such 
Indemnified Person’s actual Loss, net of any insurance or other recovery. 
 
18.1.3 Indemnity Procedures.  Promptly after receipt by an Indemnified Person 
of any claim or notice of the commencement of any action or 
administrative or legal proceeding or investigation as to which the 
indemnity provided for in Article 18.1 may apply, the Indemnified Person 
shall notify the Indemnifying Party of such fact.  Any failure of or delay in 
such notification shall not affect a Party’s indemnification obligation unless 
such failure or delay is materially prejudicial to the Indemnifying Party. 
 
The Indemnifying Party shall have the right to assume the defense thereof 
with counsel designated by such Indemnifying Party and reasonably 
satisfactory to the Indemnified Person.  If the defendants in any such 
action include one or more Indemnified Persons and the Indemnifying 
Party and if the Indemnified Person reasonably concludes that there may 
be legal defenses available to it and/or other Indemnified Persons which 
are different from or additional to those available to the Indemnifying 
Party, the Indemnified Person shall have the right to select separate 
counsel to assert such legal defenses and to otherwise participate in the 
defense of such action on its own behalf.  In such instances, the 
Indemnifying Party shall only be required to pay the fees and expenses of 
one additional attorney to represent an Indemnified Person or Indemnified 
Persons having such differing or additional legal defenses. 
 
The Indemnified Person shall be entitled, at its expense, to participate in 
any such action, suit or proceeding, the defense of which has been 
assumed by the Indemnifying Party.  Notwithstanding the foregoing, the 
Indemnifying Party (i) shall not be entitled to assume and control the 
defense of any such action, suit or proceedings if and to the extent that, in 
the opinion of the Indemnified Person and its counsel, such action, suit or 
proceeding involves the potential imposition of criminal liability on the 
Indemnified Person, or there exists a conflict or adversity of interest 
between the Indemnified Person and the Indemnifying Party, in such event 
the Indemnifying Party shall pay the reasonable expenses of the 
Indemnified Person, and (ii) shall not settle or consent to the entry of any 
judgment in any action, suit or proceeding without the consent of the 
Indemnified Person, which shall not be reasonably withheld, conditioned 
or delayed. 
 
18.2 Consequential Damages.  In no event shall either Party be liable under any 
provision of this TSRA for any losses, damages, costs or expenses for any 
special, indirect, incidental, consequential, or punitive damages, including but not 
limited to loss of profit or revenue, loss of the use of equipment, cost of capital,
--- PAGE 198 ---
Southern California Edison Company 
FERC Electric Tariff, Third Revised Volume No. 6 
 
cost of temporary equipment or services, whether based in whole or in part in 
contract, in tort, including negligence, strict liability, or any other theory of liability; 
provided, however, that damages for which a Party may be liable to the other 
Party under another agreement will not be considered to be special, indirect, 
incidental, or consequential damages hereunder. 
 
18.3 Insurance.  As indicated below, the designated Party shall, at its own expense, 
maintain in force throughout the period of this TSRA, and until released by the 
other Party, the following insurance coverages, with insurers rated no less than 
A- under A. M Bests’ current Key Rating Guides and authorized to do business in 
the state where the Point of Interconnection is located: 
 
18.3.1 Workers’ Compensation Insurance and Employers’ Liability.   The 
Participating TO and the Large Load Interconnection Customer shall 
maintain such coverage from the commencement of any construction 
activities in statutory limits for workers’ compensation coverage, and 
coverage amounts of one million dollars ($1,000,000) for employer’s 
liability for each employee for bodily injury by accident and one million 
dollars ($1,000,000) for each employee for bodily injury by disease in 
accordance with the laws and regulations of the state in which the 
Point of Interconnection is located.   
 
18.3.2 Commercial General Liability Insurance.  The Participating TO and the 
Large Load Interconnection Customer shall maintain commercial general 
liability insurance coverage commencing within thirty (30) Calendar Days 
of the Effective Date of this TSRA, including coverage for premises and 
operations, bodily injury (including death), personal injury, property 
damage, products and completed operations coverage, coverage for 
explosion, collapse and underground hazards, independent contractors 
coverage, and (i) liability of Participating TO and the Large Load 
Interconnection Customer that would be imposed without the TSRA, or (ii) 
liability assumed by the Participating TO and the Large Load 
Interconnection Customer in a contract or agreement that is an “insured 
contract” under commercial general liability insurance policy.  Such 
insurance shall include cross liability or separation of insured clause or 
endorsement. Such insurance shall be in limits of one million dollars 
($1,000,000) per occurrence and two million dollars ($2,000,000) in the 
aggregate.   
 
18.3.3 Business Automobile Liability Insurance.  Prior to the entry of any 
vehicles on any construction site in connection with work done by or on 
behalf of either Party, each shall provide evidence of coverage of owned 
and non-owned and hired vehicles, trailers or semi-trailers designed for 
travel on public roads, with a combined single limit of one million dollars 
($1,000,000) per occurrence for bodily injury, including death, and 
property damage.
--- PAGE 199 ---
Southern California Edison Company 
FERC Electric Tariff, Third Revised Volume No. 6 
 
 
18.3.4 Excess Liability Insurance.  Commencing at the time of entry of any 
person on its behalf upon any construction site for the Large Load 
Network Upgrades, Distribution Upgrades, Interconnection Facilities, or 
Large Load, the Participating TO and the Large Load Interconnection 
Customer shall maintain excess liability insurance over and above the 
Employers’ Liability, Commercial General Liability, and Business 
Automobile Liability Insurance coverage, with a limit of five million dollars 
per MW, of Large Load capacity, rounded up to the nearest MW, per 
occurrence, up to a maximum of twenty-five million dollars ($25,000,000) 
per occurrence/twenty-five million dollars ($25,000,000) aggregate.  The 
requirements of Articles 18.3.2 and 18.3.4 may be met by any combination 
of general and excess liability insurance. 
 
18.3.5 As required in this Article 18.3, the Commercial General Liability 
Insurance, Business Automobile Liability Insurance and Excess Liability 
Insurance policies shall include the other Party identified in the articles 
above, its parent, their subsidiaries, respective directors, officers, agents, 
servants and employees ("Other Party Group") as additional insured.  All 
policies shall contain provisions whereby the insurers waive all rights of 
subrogation in accordance with the provisions of this TSRA against the 
Other Party Group.  If any Party can reasonably demonstrate that 
coverage policies containing provisions for insurer waiver of subrogation 
rights, or advance notice are not commercially available, then the Parties 
shall meet and confer and mutually determine to (i) establish replacement 
or equivalent terms in lieu of subrogation or notice or (ii) waive the 
requirements that coverage(s) include such subrogation provision or 
require advance written notice from such insurers. 
 
18.3.6 As required in this Article 18.3, the Commercial General Liability Insurance 
and Business Automobile Liability Insurance policies shall contain 
provisions that specify that the policies are primary and non-contributory.  
Each Party shall be responsible for its respective deductibles or self-
insured retentions. 
 
18.3.7 As required in this Article 18.3, the Commercial General Liability 
Insurance, Business Automobile Liability Insurance and Excess Liability 
Insurance policies, if written on a Claims First Made Basis, shall be 
maintained in full force and effect for two (2) years after termination of this 
TSRA, which coverage may be in the form of extended reporting period 
coverage if agreed by the Parties. 
 
18.3.8 If the activities of the Large Load Interconnection Customer are being 
conducted through the actions of an Affiliate, then the Large Load 
Interconnection Customer may satisfy the insurance requirements of this 
Article 18.3 by providing evidence of insurance coverage carried by such
--- PAGE 200 ---
Southern California Edison Company 
FERC Electric Tariff, Third Revised Volume No. 6 
 
Affiliate, together with the Large Load Interconnection Customer’s written 
representation to the Participating TO that the insured Affiliate is 
conducting all of the necessary pre-construction work. Within thirty (30) 
calendar days prior to the entry of any person on behalf of the Large Load 
Interconnection Customer onto any construction site to perform work 
related to the Interconnection Facilities or Large Load, the Large Load 
Interconnection Customer shall replace any evidence of Affiliate insurance 
with evidence of such insurance carried by the Large Load Interconnection 
Customer.  
 
18.3.9 Thirty (30) Calendar Days prior to the start of any work at the construction 
site related to Interconnection Facilities or Large Load under this TSRA, 
and as soon as practicable after the end of each fiscal year or at the 
renewal of the insurance policy and in any event within ninety (90) 
Calendar Days thereafter, each Party shall provide a certificate of 
insurance for all insurance required in this TSRA, executed by each 
insurer or by an authorized representative of each insurer. 
 
18.3.10 Notwithstanding the foregoing, each Party may self-insure (a) to meet 
the insurance requirements of Article 18.3.1, to the extent that it maintains 
a self-insurance program and is a qualified self-insurer within the state in 
which the Point of Interconnection is located, under the laws and 
regulations of such state; and (b) to meet the insurance requirements of 
Articles 18.3.2 through 18.3.4 to the extent it maintains a self-insurance 
program; provided that, such Party is organized under the laws of the 
United States or a political subdivision thereof and such Party’s rating for 
its senior unsecured, long-term debt (not supported by third party credit 
enhancements) or if such Party does not have a rating for its senior 
unsecured long-term debt, then the rating then assigned to such Party by 
Standard & Poor’s Rating Group (a division of McGraw-Hill, Inc.) or its 
successor (“S&P”) or Moody’s Investor Services, Inc. or its successor 
(“Moody’s”) is (i) if rated by S&P and Moody’s is rated at least ”BBB-” by 
S&P and “Baa3" by Moody’s, or (ii) if rated by only one of S&P or Moody’s, 
rated at least ”BBB-” by S&P or “Baa3” by Moody’s, and (iii) that its self-
insurance program meets the insurance requirements of Articles 18.3.2 
through 18.3.4.  For any period of time that a Party’s senior unsecured, 
long-term debt is unrated by S&P or Moody’s, or its unsecured long-term 
debt or the rating assigned to such Party does not meet the requirements 
in (ii), such Party shall comply with the insurance requirements applicable 
to it under Articles 18.3.2 through 18.3.4.  In the event that a Party is 
permitted to self-insure pursuant to this Article 18.3.10, it shall notify the 
other Party that it meets the requirements to self-insure and that its self-
insurance program meets the insurance requirements in a manner 
consistent with that specified in Article 18.

18.3.11 The Parties agree to report to each other in writing as soon as practical 
whenever an accident or incident occurs resulting in serious injuries to any 
person, including death, and any third-party property damage greater than 
$500,000 arising out of this Agreement. 
 
Article 19. 
Assignment 
 
19.1 Assignment.  This TSRA may be assigned by either Party only with the written 
consent of the other; and provided further that Large Load Interconnection 
Customer shall have the right to assign this TSRA, without the consent of 
Participating TO, for collateral security purposes to aid in providing financing for 
the Large Load, provided that Large Load Interconnection Customer will promptly 
notify Participating TO of any such assignment.  Any financing arrangement 
entered into by Large Load Interconnection Customer pursuant to this Article will 
provide that prior to or upon the exercise of the secured party’s, trustee’s or 
mortgagee’s assignment rights pursuant to said arrangement, the secured 
creditor, the trustee or mortgagee will notify Participating TO of the date and 
particulars of any such exercise of assignment right(s), including providing the 
Participating TO with proof that it meets the requirements of Articles 11.5 and 
18.3.  Any attempted assignment that violates this Article is void and ineffective.  
Any assignment under this TSRA shall not relieve a Party of its obligations, nor 
shall a Party’s obligations be enlarged, in whole or in part, by reason thereof.  
Where required, consent to assignment will not be unreasonably withheld, 
conditioned or delayed. 
 
Article 20. 
Severability 
 
20.1 Severability.  If any provision in this TSRA is finally determined to be invalid, 
void or unenforceable by any court or other Governmental Authority having 
jurisdiction, such determination shall not invalidate, void or make unenforceable 
any other provision, agreement or covenant of this TSRA. 
 
Article 21. 
Comparability 
 
21.1 Comparability.  The Parties will comply with all applicable comparability and 
code of conduct laws, rules and regulations, as amended from time to time. 
 
Article 22. 
Confidentiality 
 
22.1 Confidentiality.  Confidential Information shall include, without limitation, all 
information relating to a Party’s technology, research and development, business 
affairs, and pricing, and any information supplied by either of the Parties to the 
other prior to the execution of this TSRA. 
 
Information is Confidential Information only if it is clearly designated or marked in 
writing as confidential on the face of the document, or, if the information is
--- PAGE 202 ---
Southern California Edison Company 
FERC Electric Tariff, Third Revised Volume No. 6 
 
conveyed orally or by inspection, if the Party providing the information orally 
informs the Party receiving the information that the information is confidential. 
 
If requested by either Party, the other Party shall provide in writing, the basis for 
asserting that the information referred to in this Article 22 warrants confidential 
treatment, and the requesting Party may disclose such writing to the appropriate 
Governmental Authority.  Each Party shall be responsible for the costs 
associated with affording confidential treatment to its information. 
 
22.1.1 Term.  During the term of this TSRA, and for a period of three (3) years 
after the expiration or termination of this TSRA, except as otherwise 
provided in this Article 22, each Party shall hold in confidence and shall 
not disclose to any person Confidential Information. 
 
22.1.2 Scope.  Confidential Information shall not include information that the 
receiving Party can demonstrate: (1) is generally available to the public 
other than as a result of a disclosure by the receiving Party; (2) was in the 
lawful possession of the receiving Party on a non-confidential basis before 
receiving it from the disclosing Party; (3) was supplied to the receiving 
Party without restriction by a third party, who, to the knowledge of the 
receiving Party after due inquiry, was under no obligation to the disclosing 
Party to keep such information confidential; (4) was independently 
developed by the receiving Party without reference to Confidential 
Information of the disclosing Party; (5) is, or becomes, publicly known, 
through no wrongful act or omission of the receiving Party or Breach of 
this TSRA; or (6) is required, in accordance with Article 22.1.7 of the 
TSRA, Order of Disclosure, to be disclosed by any Governmental 
Authority or is otherwise required to be disclosed by law or subpoena, or is 
necessary in any legal proceeding establishing rights and obligations 
under this TSRA.  Information designated as Confidential Information will 
no longer be deemed confidential if the Party that designated the 
information as confidential notifies the other Party that it no longer is 
confidential. 
 
22.1.3 Release of Confidential Information.  Neither Party shall release or 
disclose Confidential Information to any other person, except to its 
Affiliates (limited by the Standards of Conduct requirements), 
subcontractors, employees, consultants, or to parties who may be or 
considering providing financing to or equity participation with Large Load 
Interconnection Customer, or to potential purchasers or assignees of 
Large Load Interconnection Customer, on a need-to-know basis in 
connection with this TSRA, unless such person has first been advised of 
the confidentiality provisions of this Article 22 and has agreed to comply 
with such provisions.  Notwithstanding the foregoing, a Party providing 
Confidential Information to any person shall remain primarily responsible
--- PAGE 203 ---
Southern California Edison Company 
FERC Electric Tariff, Third Revised Volume No. 6 
 
for any release of Confidential Information in contravention of this Article 
22. 
 
22.1.4 Rights.  Each Party retains all rights, title, and interest in the Confidential 
Information that each Party discloses to the other Party.  The disclosure 
by each Party to the other Party of Confidential Information shall not be 
deemed a waiver by either Party or any other person or entity of the right 
to protect the Confidential Information from public disclosure. 
 
22.1.5 No Warranties.  By providing Confidential Information, neither Party 
makes any warranties or representations as to its accuracy or 
completeness.  In addition, by supplying Confidential Information, neither 
Party obligates itself to provide any particular information or Confidential 
Information to the other Party nor to enter into any further agreements or 
proceed with any other relationship or joint venture. 
 
22.1.6 Standard of Care.  Each Party shall use at least the same standard of 
care to protect Confidential Information it receives as it uses to protect its 
own Confidential Information from unauthorized disclosure, publication or 
dissemination.  Each Party may use Confidential Information solely to fulfill 
its obligations to the other Party under this TSRA or its regulatory 
requirements. 
 
22.1.7 Order of Disclosure.  If a court or a Government Authority or entity with 
the right, power, and apparent authority to do so requests or requires 
either Party, by subpoena, oral deposition, interrogatories, requests for 
production of documents, administrative order, or otherwise, to disclose 
Confidential Information, that Party shall provide the other Party with 
prompt notice of such request(s) or requirement(s) so that the other Party 
may seek an appropriate protective order or waive compliance with the 
terms of this TSRA.  Notwithstanding the absence of a protective order or 
waiver, the Party may disclose such Confidential Information which, in the 
opinion of its counsel, the Party is legally compelled to disclose.  Each 
Party will use Reasonable Efforts to obtain reliable assurance that 
confidential treatment will be accorded any Confidential Information so 
furnished. 
 
22.1.8 Termination of Agreement.  Upon termination of this TSRA for any 
reason, each Party shall, within ten (10) Calendar Days of receipt of a 
written request from the other Party, use Reasonable Efforts to destroy, 
erase, or delete (with such destruction, erasure, and deletion certified in 
writing to the other Party) or return to the other Party, without retaining 
copies thereof, any and all written or electronic Confidential Information 
received from the other Party.
--- PAGE 204 ---
Southern California Edison Company 
FERC Electric Tariff, Third Revised Volume No. 6 
 
22.1.9 Remedies.  The Parties agree that monetary damages would be 
inadequate to compensate a Party for the other Party’s Breach of its 
obligations under this Article 22.  Each Party accordingly agrees that the 
other Party shall be entitled to equitable relief, by way of injunction or 
otherwise, if the first Party Breaches or threatens to Breach its obligations 
under this Article 22, which equitable relief shall be granted without bond 
or proof of damages, and the receiving Party shall not plead in defense 
that there would be an adequate remedy at law.  Such remedy shall not be 
deemed an exclusive remedy for the Breach of this Article 22, but shall be 
in addition to all other remedies available at law or in equity.  The Parties 
further acknowledge and agree that the covenants contained herein are 
necessary for the protection of legitimate business interests and are 
reasonable in scope.  No Party, however, shall be liable for indirect, 
incidental, or consequential or punitive damages of any nature or kind 
resulting from or arising in connection with this Article 22. 
 
22.1.10 Disclosure to FERC, its Staff, or a State.  Notwithstanding anything in 
this Article 22 to the contrary, and pursuant to 18 CFR section 1b.20, if 
FERC or its staff, during the course of an investigation or otherwise, 
requests information from one of the Parties that is otherwise required to 
be maintained in confidence pursuant to this TSRA, the Party shall provide 
the requested information to FERC or its staff, within the time provided for 
in the request for information.  In providing the information to FERC or its 
staff, the Party must, consistent with 18 CFR section 388.112, request that 
the information be treated as confidential and non-public by FERC and its 
staff and that the information be withheld from public disclosure.  Parties 
are prohibited from notifying the other Party to this TSRA prior to the 
release of the Confidential Information to FERC or its staff.  The Party 
shall notify the other Party to the TSRA when it is notified by FERC or its 
staff that a request to release Confidential Information has been received 
by FERC, at which time either of the Parties may respond before such 
information would be made public, pursuant to 18 CFR section 388.112.  
Requests from a state regulatory body conducting a confidential 
investigation shall be treated in a similar manner if consistent with the 
applicable state rules and regulations. 
 
22.1.11 Subject to the exception in Article 22.1.10, any information that a Party 
claims is competitively sensitive, commercial or financial information under 
this TSRA ("Confidential Information") shall not be disclosed by the other 
Party to any person not employed or retained by the other Party, except to 
the extent disclosure is (i) required by law; (ii) reasonably deemed by the 
disclosing Party to be required to be disclosed in connection with a dispute 
between or among the Parties, or the defense of litigation or dispute; (iii) 
otherwise permitted by consent of the other Party, such consent not to be 
unreasonably withheld; or (iv) necessary to fulfill its obligations under this 
TSRA or as a transmission service provider or a Balancing Authority Area
--- PAGE 205 ---
Southern California Edison Company 
FERC Electric Tariff, Third Revised Volume No. 6 
 
operator including disclosing the Confidential Information to an RTO or 
ISO or to a regional or national reliability organization.  The Party 
asserting confidentiality shall notify the other Party in writing of the 
information it claims is confidential.  Prior to any disclosures of the other 
Party’s Confidential Information under this subparagraph, or if any third 
party or Governmental Authority makes any request or demand for any of 
the information described in this subparagraph, the disclosing Party 
agrees to promptly notify the other Party in writing and agrees to assert 
confidentiality and cooperate with the other Party in seeking to protect the 
Confidential Information from public disclosure by confidentiality 
agreement, protective order or other reasonable measures. 
 
Article 23. 
Environmental Releases 
 
23.1  Each Party shall notify the other Party, first orally and then in writing, of the 
release of any Hazardous Substances, any asbestos or lead abatement 
activities, or any type of remediation activities related to the Large Load or the 
Interconnection Facilities, each of which may reasonably be expected to affect 
the other Party.  The notifying Party shall: (i) provide the notice as soon as 
practicable, provided such Party makes a good faith effort to provide the notice 
no later than twenty-four hours after such Party becomes aware of the 
occurrence; and (ii) promptly furnish to the other Party copies of any publicly 
available reports filed with any Governmental Authorities addressing such 
events. 
 
Article 24. 
Information Requirements 
 
24.1 Information Acquisition.  Participating TO and Large Load Interconnection 
Customer shall submit specific information regarding the electrical characteristics 
of their respective facilities to each other as described below and in accordance 
with Applicable Reliability Standards. 
 
24.2 Information Submission by Participating TO.  The initial information 
submission by Participating TO shall occur no later than one hundred eighty 
(180) Calendar Days prior to Service Commencement Date and shall include 
transmission system information necessary to allow Large Load Interconnection 
Customer to select equipment and meet any system protection and stability 
requirements, unless otherwise agreed to by the Parties.  On a monthly basis 
Participating TO shall provide Large Load Interconnection Customer a status 
report on the construction and installation of Participating TO’s Interconnection 
Facilities, Distribution Upgrades, and Large Load Network Upgrades, including, 
but not limited to, the following information: (1) progress to date; (2) a description 
of the activities since the last report; (3) a description of the action items for the 
next period; and (4) the delivery status of equipment ordered.
--- PAGE 206 ---
Southern California Edison Company 
FERC Electric Tariff, Third Revised Volume No. 6 
 
24.3 Updated Information Submission by Large Load Interconnection Customer.  
The updated information submission by Large Load Interconnection Customer, 
including manufacturer information, shall occur no later than one hundred eighty 
(180) Calendar Days prior to the Service Commencement Date.  Large Load 
Interconnection Customer shall submit a completed copy of the Large Load data 
requirements contained in Appendix 1 to the LLIP.  It shall also include any 
additional information provided to Participating TO for the Large Load 
Interconnection Study.  Information in this submission shall be the most current 
Large Load design or expected performance data.  Information submitted for 
stability models shall be compatible with Participating TO standard models.  If 
there is no compatible model, Large Load Interconnection Customer will work 
with a consultant mutually agreed to by the Parties to develop and supply a 
standard model and associated information. 
 
If Large Load Interconnection Customer’s data is materially different from what 
was originally provided to Participating TO pursuant to the Large Load Study 
Process Agreement between Participating TO and Large Load Interconnection 
Customer, then Participating TO will conduct appropriate studies to determine 
the impact on Participating TO’s Transmission System and Distribution System 
based on the actual data submitted pursuant to this Article 24.3.  The Large Load 
Interconnection Customer shall not energize or operate the Large Load or co-
located generation, if applicable, in parallel with the Participating TO’s 
Transmission System until such studies are completed. Changes not previously 
studied may require additional scope, may delay the Service Commencement 
Date, or, if determined by Participating TO to constitute a Material Modification, 
will require Large Load Interconnection Customer to submit a new 
Interconnection Request pursuant to the LLIP.  
 
24.4 Information Supplementation.  Prior to the Service Commencement Date, the 
Parties shall supplement their information submissions described above in this 
Article 24 with any and all “as-built” Large Load information or “as-tested” 
performance information that differs from the initial submissions or, alternatively, 
written confirmation that no such differences exist.  The Large Load 
Interconnection Customer shall conduct tests on the Large Load as required by 
Good Utility Practice.  
 
Subsequent to the Service Commencement Date, Large Load Interconnection 
Customer shall provide Participating TO any information changes due to 
equipment replacement, repair, or adjustment.  Participating TO shall provide 
Large Load Interconnection Customer any information changes due to equipment 
replacement, repair or adjustment in the directly connected substation or any 
adjacent Participating TO-owned substation that may affect Large Load 
Interconnection Customer’s Interconnection Facilities equipment ratings, 
protection or operating requirements.  The Parties shall provide such information 
no later than thirty (30) Calendar Days after the date of the equipment 
replacement, repair or adjustment.

Southern California Edison Company 
FERC Electric Tariff, Third Revised Volume No. 6 
 
 
Article 25. 
Information Access and Audit Rights 
 
25.1 Information Access.  Each Party (the “disclosing Party”) shall make available to 
the other Party information that is in the possession of the disclosing Party and is 
necessary in order for the other Party to: (i) verify the costs incurred by the 
disclosing Party for which the other Party is responsible under this TSRA; and (ii) 
carry out its obligations and responsibilities under this TSRA.  The Parties shall 
not use such information for purposes other than those set forth in this Article 
25.1 and to enforce their rights under this TSRA. 
 
25.2 Reporting of Non-Uncontrollable Force Events.  Each Party (the “notifying 
Party”) shall notify the other Party when the notifying Party becomes aware of its 
inability to comply with the provisions of this TSRA for a reason other than an 
Uncontrollable Force event.  The Parties agree to cooperate with each other and 
provide necessary information regarding such inability to comply, including the 
date, duration, reason for the inability to comply, and corrective actions taken or 
planned to be taken with respect to such inability to comply.  Notwithstanding the 
foregoing, notification, cooperation or information provided under this Article shall 
not entitle the Party receiving such notification to allege a cause for anticipatory 
breach of this TSRA. 
 
25.3 Audit Rights.  Subject to the requirements of confidentiality under Article 22 of 
this TSRA, each Party shall have the right, during normal business hours, and 
upon prior reasonable notice to the other Party, to audit at its own expense the 
other Party’s accounts and records pertaining to either Party’s performance or 
either Party’s satisfaction of obligations under this TSRA.  Such audit rights shall 
include audits of the other Party’s costs, calculation of invoiced amounts, and 
each Party’s actions in an Emergency Condition.  Any audit authorized by this 
Article shall be performed at the offices where such accounts and records are 
maintained and shall be limited to those portions of such accounts and records 
that relate to each Party’s performance and satisfaction of obligations under this 
TSRA.  Each Party shall keep such accounts and records for a period equivalent 
to the audit rights periods described in Article 25.4. 
 
25.4 Audit Rights Periods. 
 
25.4.1 Audit Rights Period for Construction-Related Accounts and Records.  
Accounts and records related to the design, engineering, procurement, 
and construction of Participating TO’s Interconnection Facilities, 
Distribution Upgrades, and Large Load Network Upgrades shall be subject 
to audit for a period of twenty-four months following Participating TO’s 
issuance of a final invoice in accordance with Article 12.2. 
 
25.4.2 Audit Rights Period for All Other Accounts and Records.  Accounts 
and records related to either Party’s performance or satisfaction of all
--- PAGE 208 ---
Southern California Edison Company 
FERC Electric Tariff, Third Revised Volume No. 6 
 
obligations under this TSRA other than those described in Article 25.4.1 
shall be subject to audit as follows:  (i) for an audit relating to cost 
obligations, the applicable audit rights period shall be twenty-four months 
after the auditing Party’s receipt of an invoice giving rise to such cost 
obligations; and (ii) for an audit relating to all other obligations, the 
applicable audit rights period shall be twenty-four months after the event 
for which the audit is sought. 
 
25.5 Audit Results.  If an audit by a Party determines that an overpayment or an 
underpayment has occurred, a notice of such overpayment or underpayment 
shall be given to the other Party together with those records from the audit which 
support such determination.   
 
Article 26. 
Subcontractors 
 
26.1 General.  Nothing in this TSRA shall prevent a Party from utilizing the services of 
any subcontractor as it deems appropriate to perform its obligations under this 
TSRA; provided, however, that each Party shall require its subcontractors to 
comply with all applicable terms and conditions of this TSRA in providing such 
services and each Party shall remain primarily liable to the other Party for the 
performance of such subcontractor. 
 
26.2 Responsibility of Principal.  The creation of any subcontract relationship shall 
not relieve the hiring Party of any of its obligations under this TSRA.  The hiring 
Party shall be fully responsible to the other Party for the acts or omissions of any 
subcontractor the hiring Party hires as if no subcontract had been made; 
provided, however, that in no event shall Participating TO be liable for the actions 
or inactions of Large Load Interconnection Customer or its subcontractors with 
respect to obligations of Large Load Interconnection Customer under Article 5 of 
this TSRA.  Any applicable obligation imposed by this TSRA upon the hiring 
Party shall be equally binding upon, and shall be construed as having application 
to, any subcontractor of such Party. 
 
26.3 No Limitation by Insurance.  The obligations under this Article 26 will not be 
limited in any way by any limitation of subcontractor’s insurance. 
 
Article 27. 
Disputes  
 
27.1 Submission.  In the event either Party has a dispute, or asserts a claim, which 
arises out of or in connection with the TSRA, the LLIP, or their performance, such 
Party (the "disputing Party") shall provide the other Party with written notice of 
the dispute or claim ("Notice of Dispute").  Such dispute or claim shall be 
resolved in accordance with the Dispute Resolution Procedures set forth in 
Section 14 of the TO Tariff.  
 
Article 28. 
Representations, Warranties, and Covenants
--- PAGE 209 ---
Southern California Edison Company 
FERC Electric Tariff, Third Revised Volume No. 6 
 
 
28.1 General.  Each Party makes the following representations, warranties and 
covenants:  
 
28.1.1 Good Standing.  Such Party is duly organized, validly existing and in 
good standing under the laws of the state in which it is organized, formed, 
or incorporated, as applicable; that it is qualified to do business in the 
state or states in which the Large Load, Interconnection Facilities and 
Large Load Network Upgrades owned by such Party, as applicable, are 
located; and that it has the corporate power and authority to own its 
properties, to carry on its business as now being conducted and to enter 
into this TSRA and carry out the transactions contemplated hereby and 
perform and carry out all covenants and obligations on its part to be 
performed under and pursuant to this TSRA. 
 
28.1.2 Authority.  Such Party has the right, power and authority to enter into this 
TSRA, to become a Party hereto and to perform its obligations hereunder.  
This TSRA is a legal, valid and binding obligation of such Party, 
enforceable against such Party in accordance with its terms, except as the 
enforceability thereof may be limited by applicable bankruptcy, insolvency, 
reorganization or other similar laws affecting creditors’ rights generally and 
by general equitable principles (regardless of whether enforceability is 
sought in a proceeding in equity or at law). 
 
 
28.1.3 No Conflict.  The execution, delivery and performance of this TSRA does 
not violate or conflict with the organizational or formation documents, or 
bylaws or operating agreement, of such Party, or any judgment, license, 
permit, order, material agreement or instrument applicable to or binding 
upon such Party or any of its assets. 
 
28.1.4 Consent and Approval.  Such Party has sought or obtained, or, in 
accordance with this TSRA will seek or obtain, each consent, approval, 
authorization, order, or acceptance by any Governmental Authority in 
connection with the execution, delivery and performance of this TSRA, 
and it will provide to any Governmental Authority notice of any actions 
under this TSRA that are required by Applicable Laws and Regulations. 
 
Article 29. 
Miscellaneous 
 
29.1 Binding Effect.  This TSRA and the rights and obligations hereof, shall be 
binding upon and shall inure to the benefit of the successors and assigns of the 
Parties hereto. 
 
29.2 Conflicts.  In the event of a conflict between the body of this TSRA and any 
attachment, appendices or exhibits hereto, the terms and provisions of the body 
of this TSRA shall prevail and be deemed the final intent of the Parties.
--- PAGE 210 ---
Southern California Edison Company 
FERC Electric Tariff, Third Revised Volume No. 6 
 
 
29.3 Rules of Interpretation.  This TSRA, unless a clear contrary intention appears, 
shall be construed and interpreted as follows:  (1) the singular number includes 
the plural number and vice versa;  (2) reference to any person includes such 
person’s successors and assigns but, in the case of a Party, only if such 
successors and assigns are permitted by this TSRA, and reference to a person in 
a particular capacity excludes such person in any other capacity or individually; 
(3) reference to any agreement (including this TSRA), document, instrument or 
tariff means such agreement, document, instrument, or tariff as amended or 
modified and in effect from time to time in accordance with the terms thereof and, 
if applicable, the terms hereof; (4) reference to any Applicable Laws and 
Regulations means such Applicable Laws and Regulations as amended, 
modified, codified, or reenacted, in whole or in part, and in effect from time to 
time, including, if applicable, rules and regulations promulgated thereunder; (5) 
unless expressly stated otherwise, reference to any Article, Section or Appendix 
means such Article of this TSRA or such Appendix to this TSRA, or such Section 
to the LLIP or such Appendix to the LLIP, as the case may be; (6) “hereunder”, 
“hereof”, “herein”, “hereto” and words of similar import shall be deemed 
references to this TSRA as a whole and not to any particular Article or other 
provision hereof or thereof; (7) “including” (and with correlative meaning 
“include”) means including without limiting the generality of any description 
preceding such term; and (8) relative to the determination of any period of time, 
“from” means “from and including”, “to” means “to but excluding” and “through” 
means “through and including”. 
 
29.4 Entire Agreement.  This TSRA, including all Appendices and Schedules 
attached hereto, constitutes the entire agreement between the Parties with 
reference to the subject matter hereof, and supersedes all prior and 
contemporaneous understandings or agreements, oral or written, between the 
Parties with respect to the subject matter of this TSRA.  There are no other 
agreements, representations, warranties, or covenants which constitute any part 
of the consideration for, or any condition to, either Party’s compliance with its 
obligations under this TSRA. 
 
29.5 No Third Party Beneficiaries.  This TSRA is not intended to and does not 
create rights, remedies, or benefits of any character whatsoever in favor of any 
persons, corporations, associations, or entities other than the Parties, and the 
obligations herein assumed are solely for the use and benefit of the Parties, their 
successors in interest and, where permitted, their assigns. 
 
29.6 Waiver.  The failure of a Party to this TSRA to insist, on any occasion, upon strict 
performance of any provision of this TSRA will not be considered a waiver of any 
obligation, right, or duty of, or imposed upon, such Party. 
 
Any waiver at any time by either Party of its rights with respect to this TSRA shall 
not be deemed a continuing waiver or a waiver with respect to any other failure to
--- PAGE 211 ---
Southern California Edison Company 
FERC Electric Tariff, Third Revised Volume No. 6 
 
comply with any other obligation, right, duty of this TSRA.  Termination or Default 
of this TSRA for any reason by Large Load Interconnection Customer shall not 
constitute a waiver of Large Load Interconnection Customer’s legal rights to 
obtain an interconnection from Participating TO.  Any waiver of this TSRA shall, if 
requested, be provided in writing. 
 
29.7 Headings.  The descriptive headings of the various articles of this TSRA have 
been inserted for convenience of reference only and are of no significance in the 
interpretation or construction of this TSRA. 
 
29.8 Multiple Counterparts.  This TSRA may be executed in two or more 
counterparts, each of which is deemed an original but all constitute one and the 
same instrument. 
 
29.9 Amendment.  The Parties may by mutual agreement amend this TSRA by a 
written instrument duly executed by the Parties. 
 
29.10 Modification by the Parties.  The Parties may by mutual agreement amend the 
Appendices to this TSRA by a written instrument duly executed by the Parties.  
Such amendment shall become effective and a part of this TSRA upon 
satisfaction of all Applicable Laws and Regulations. 
 
29.11 Reservation of Rights.  Participating TO shall have the right to make a 
unilateral filing with FERC to modify this TSRA with respect to any rates, terms 
and conditions, charges, classifications of service, rule or regulation under 
section 205 or any other applicable provision of the Federal Power Act and 
FERC’s rules and regulations thereunder, and Large Load Interconnection 
Customer shall have the right to make a unilateral filing with FERC to modify this 
TSRA pursuant to section 206 or any other applicable provision of the Federal 
Power Act and FERC’s rules and regulations thereunder; provided that each 
Party shall have the right to protest any such filing by the other Party and to 
participate fully in any proceeding before FERC in which such modifications may 
be considered.  Nothing in this TSRA shall limit the rights of the Parties or of 
FERC under sections 205 or 206 of the Federal Power Act and FERC’s rules and 
regulations thereunder, except to the extent that the Parties otherwise mutually 
agree as provided herein. 
 
29.12 No Partnership.  This TSRA shall not be interpreted or construed to create an 
association, joint venture, agency relationship, or partnership between the 
Parties or to impose any partnership obligation or partnership liability upon either 
Party.  Neither Party shall have any right, power or authority to enter into any 
agreement or undertaking for, or act on behalf of, or to act as or be an agent or 
representative of, or to otherwise bind, the other Party.

Appendix B to TSRA 
 
Requirements for Mitigation of Potential Cost Shifting Related to New 
Transmission Facilities 
 
 
1. Security For Large Load Network Upgrades and ISO-Identified Network 
Upgrades 
a. Initial Amount of Security.  For the Large Load Network Upgrades 
identified in Appendix A and any ISO-Identified Network Upgrades, Large 
Load Interconnection Customer must provide Interconnection Financial 
Security equal to the Participating TO’s estimated costs attributable to the 
applicable facilities, including engineering, design, procurement, 
permitting, construction, installation, testing, and commissioning costs, 
together with applicable labor, materials, contractor charges, and 
overhead.  If the final engineering and design indicates a change in the 
scope of Large Load Network Upgrades or ISO-Identified Network 
Upgrades, the costs of the incremental resulting Large Load Network 
Upgrades and ISO-Identified Network Upgrades shall be included as 
additional Interconnection Financial Security. 
i. Large Load Network Upgrades and ISO-Identified Network 
Upgrades Interconnection Financial Security: ____________ 
b. Reduction in Amount of Security.  Large Load Interconnection 
Customer is entitled to annually reduce the Interconnection Financial 
Security identified in Section 1.a, in accordance with this Section.  
Reductions shall commence once the Large Load commences retail 
service.  The amount reduced against the total security for the Large Load 
Network Upgrades and any ISO-Identified Network Upgrades will equal 
75% multiplied by the amount of MWh counted as Gross Load of the 
Large Load Interconnection Customer associated with withdrawals by the 
Large Load multiplied by the Regional Access Charge.  The Regional 
Access Charge will be set for purposes of this calculation in the same 
manner as set in Section 2.c of this Appendix B and will be fixed once set.  
In order to receive this reduction, the Large Load Interconnection 
Customer will inform the Participating TO of its Gross Load withdrawn as 
measured by the ISO revenue meter.  Any remaining unreduced balance 
of Interconnection Financial Security for the Large Load Network 
Upgrades and any ISO-Identified Network Upgrades after fifteen (15) 
years will be drawn down by Participating TO and credited under 
Participating TO’s Transmission Revenue Balancing Account Adjustment. 
2. Minimum Annual Revenue Obligation
--- PAGE 215 ---
Southern California Edison Company 
FERC Electric Tariff, Third Revised Volume No. 6 
 
a. Calculation of Minimum Annual Revenue Obligation 
i. The Minimum Annual Revenue Obligation (“MARO”) is an amount 
of revenue that will be collected in each yearly period to ensure that 
the Large Load Interconnection Customer contributes sufficiently to 
the Gross Load in the ISO Balancing Authority Area, or else pays a 
shortfall amount that will flow through the relevant revenue 
requirement (used to set the Regional Access Charge and Regional 
Wheeling Access Charge and, if relevant, used to set the Local 
Access Charge and Local Wheeling Access Charge) in accordance 
with the ISO Tariff.  “Regional Access” Charge and “Regional 
Wheeling Access Charge” are all defined in the ISO Tariff.   
ii. For each yearly period, Large Load Interconnection Customer’s 
Minimum Annual Revenue Obligation shall be calculated as follows: 
Minimum Annual Revenue Obligation = Minimum Obligation Capacity (MW) × [hours in 
the year] × 80% × 75% × Regional Access Charge (if connected to High Voltage 
facilities) 
iii. The Regional Access Charge or the Regional Access Charge plus 
the Local Access Charge amount will be fixed at the most recent 
rates posted by the ISO as of the date of execution for the fifteen 
years that the Minimum Annual Revenue Obligation remains in 
effect.  The ISO posts the relevant rates (using the term “TAC” 
rather than the term “RAC” on the “Settlements” page of its website 
(currently https://www.ISO.com/market-operations/settlements).   
iv. “Requested Maximum Load” means the ultimate peak demand, 
expressed in MW, specified by the Large Load Interconnection 
Customer on behalf of the Large Load in the application process in 
the LLIP.  For purposes of calculating the Minimum Annual 
Revenue Obligation during the load ramp, “Large Load Capacity” 
means the MW amount assigned to the applicable Year in the 
schedule below.  The Large Load Capacity shall reflect the 
expected maximum capacity during the relevant annual period.  
The Large Load Capacity should be set at the Requested Maximum 
Load no later than Year 5.   
Table 1. Minimum Capacity Schedule

b. Minimum Annual Revenue Obligation Reconciliation Process 
i. After each year, Participating TO shall compare the Minimum 
Annual Revenue Obligation with the annual wholesale revenues 
associated with the Gross Load of the Large Load, as measured by 
the ISO revenue meter (“Annual LL Gross Load Revenue”).  The 
Annual LL Gross Load Revenue would be calculated by the Large 
Load Interconnection Customer providing Participating TO the 
Gross Load of the Large Load End User and multiplying that 
amount by the Regional Access Charge only, or the Regional 
Access Charge plus Local Access Charge, as appropriate.  The 
same Regional Access Charge and/or Local Access Charge rates 
used to calculate the Minimum Annual Revenue Obligation will be 
used to calculate the Annual LL Gross Load Revenue.  If a Large 
Load is a retail transmission customer of the Participating TO, then 
its Annual LL Gross Load Revenue shall be equal to its retail 
transmission revenues.  If the Annual LL Gross Load Revenue for 
the annual period is greater than Minimum Annual Revenue 
Obligation, the Annual LL Gross Load Revenue calculation need 
not be performed and no reconciliation will occur because the 
Minimum Annual Revenue Obligation has been met. 
ii. If the Annual LL Gross Load Revenue is less than the Minimum 
Annual Revenue Obligation, Participating TO shall invoice the 
Large Load Interconnection Customer for the difference, payable 
within thirty (30) calendar days of receipt (“MARO Shortfall”).  
Payment of the MARO Shortfall shall satisfy the Minimum Annual 
Revenue Obligation under this Section for that year.  Any MARO 
Shortfalls paid by the Large Load Interconnection Customer would 
be treated as revenue in Participating TO’s Transmission Revenue 
Balancing Account Adjustment.  
iii. A delay in achieving the Requested Maximum Load shall not, by 
itself, postpone a scheduled increase in Minimum Annual Revenue
--- PAGE 217 ---
Southern California Edison Company 
FERC Electric Tariff, Third Revised Volume No. 6 
 
Obligation.  The Parties may change the Minimum Capacity 
Schedule only by written amendment to this TSRA, subject to any 
required regulatory approval.  The number of amendments to the 
Large Load Capacity Schedule are limited to one annually for the 
first four years after execution of the TSRA, with no amendments 
thereafter.   
c. MARO Interconnection Financial Security 
i. Provision of Interconnection Financial Security to Secure 
MARO.  The Large Load Interconnection Customer shall provide 
additional Interconnection Financial Security separate and apart 
from the Interconnection Financial Security described in Section 1 
of this Appendix B to secure its MARO.  
ii. Amount of MARO Interconnection Financial Security.  The 
Interconnection Financial Security to secure the MARO shall be 
maintained for as long as the Minimum Annual Revenue Obligation 
remains in effect, including any period necessary to resolve and 
pay a final annual reconciliation.  The required amount of 
Interconnection Financial Security shall equal five years times the 
Minimum Annual Revenue Obligation calculated using the full 
Large Load Capacity, rather than the Minimum Obligation Capacity 
applicable during the load ramp.   
1. MARO Interconnection Financial Security: __________ 
iii. Reduction and Release of MARO Interconnection Financial 
Security.  In each of the Years 10-15 of this TSRA, Large Load 
Interconnection Customer may reduce its Interconnection Financial 
Security under this Appendix B, Section 2.c by one year’s Minimum 
Annual Revenue Obligation.  Thirty days after the expiration of this 
TSRA, if all amounts due have been paid, the Participating TO will 
release the remaining MARO Interconnection Financial Security. 
d. Failure to Pay MARO Shortfall 
i. Participating TO shall draw on the MARO Interconnection Financial 
Security for any MARO Shortfall that remains unpaid more than 
sixty (60) Calendar Days after its due date.  Participating TO shall 
give Large Load Interconnection Customer written notice of the 
amount drawn and apply the proceeds to the unpaid obligation.  A 
draw does not discharge Large Load Interconnection Customer’s 
obligation to pay amounts exceeding the proceeds or relieve Large 
Load Interconnection Customer of future payment obligations.  
Within thirty (30) Calendar Days after a draw, Large Load 
Interconnection Customer shall restore the available MARO
--- PAGE 218 ---
Southern California Edison Company 
FERC Electric Tariff, Third Revised Volume No. 6 
 
Interconnection Financial Security to the required amount in 
Section 2(c)ii. 
3. Withdrawal Penalty; Exit Fee 
a. Requirements Prior to Commencement of Service.  Prior to the Service 
Commencement Date, the provisions of this Section apply. 
i. Withdrawal Penalty.  In accordance with LLIP Section 3.10.1(c), if 
Large Load Interconnection Customer fails to take service on or 
before its Service Commencement Date, the Large Load 
Interconnection Customer’s Withdrawal Penalty will be the greater 
of twenty percent (20%) of its estimated Large Load Network 
Upgrade costs as reflected in the most recently completed 
Interconnection Study, or the actual cost of any Large Load 
Network Upgrade abandoned facilities. 
b. Requirements Following Commencement of Service.  Following the 
Service Commencement Date, the provisions of this Section apply. 
i. Exit Fee.  If the TSRA is terminated prior to the expiration of the 
Term, Large Load Interconnection Customer shall pay an Exit Fee 
equal to the value of the required remaining Interconnection 
Financial Security under this Appendix B on the date of termination, 
including all remaining Interconnection Financial Security for Large 
Load Network Upgrades, ISO-Identified Network Upgrades, and 
Large Load Interconnection Customer’s MARO.  Such Exit Fee will 
be due thirty (30) Business Days after an invoice for the Exit Fee 
has been sent.  If the Large Load Interconnection Customer does 
not pay the Exit Fee in full within thirty (30) Business Days, the 
Participating TO may draw on all Interconnection Financial Security 
to pay the Exit Fee.  If this agreement is terminated, the Large Load 
Interconnection Customer’s right to Transmission Interconnection 
Service is terminated and the Large Load Interconnection 
Customer may be rendered incapable of delivering electric energy 
for consumption by the Large Load End User.

Appendix F to TSRA 
 
Security Arrangements Details 
 
 
Infrastructure security of Participating TO’s Transmission System and Distribution 
System equipment and operations and control hardware and software is essential to 
ensure day-to-day reliability and operational security.  FERC will expect the ISO, all 
transmission providers, market participants, and Large Load Interconnection Customers 
interconnected to the Participating TO’s Transmission System to comply with the 
recommendations offered by the President’s Critical Infrastructure Protection Board 
and, eventually, best practice recommendations from the electric reliability authority.  All 
public utilities will be expected to meet basic standards for system infrastructure and 
operational security, including physical, operational, and cyber-security practices.
